Kähler-type embeddings of balls into symplectic manifolds
Michael Entov,
Misha Verbitsky11
1
Partially supported by HSE University Basic Research Program,
FAPERJ E-26/202.912/2018 and CNPq - Process 310952/2021-2.
Contents
Abstract
Consider a symplectic embedding of a disjoint union of domains (lying in the standard symplectic ℝ2​n{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}) into a symplectic manifold MM.
We say that such
an embedding is Kähler-type, or respectively tame, if it is holomorphic with respect to some (not a priori fixed, Kähler-type)
integrable
complex structure on MM compatible with the symplectic form, or respectively tamed by it. Assume that MM is either of the following: a complex projective space (with the standard symplectic form);
an even-dimensional torus, or a K3 surface, equipped with an irrational Kähler-type symplectic form. Then any two Kähler-type embeddings of a disjoint union of balls into MM can be mapped into each other by a symplectomorphism acting trivially on homology. If the embeddings are holomorphic with respect to complex structures compatible with the symplectic form and lying in the same connected component of the space of Kähler-type complex structures on MM, then
the symplectomorphism can be chosen to be smoothly isotopic to the identity. For certain MM and certain disjoint unions of balls we describe precisely the obstructions to the existence of Kähler-type embeddings of the balls into MM. In particular, symplectic volume is the only obstruction for the existence of Kähler-type embeddings of lnl^{n} equal balls (for any ll) into ℂ​Pn{\mathbb{C}}P^{n} with the standard symplectic form and of any number of possibly different balls into a torus or a K3 surface, equipped with an irrational symplectic form.
We also show that symplectic volume is the only obstruction for the existence of tame embeddings of disjoint unions of equal balls, polydisks, or parallelepipeds, into a torus equipped with a generic Kähler-type symplectic form. For balls and parallelepipeds the same is true for K3 surfaces.
1 Introduction
Symplectic embeddings of balls and other domains into symplectic manifolds have been studied since the early days of symplectic topology.
So far, the existence of the embeddings (the so-called symplectic packing problem) got most of attention and much less is known
about the connectedness of the spaces of such embeddings. The notable exception
is the classical result of McDuff [McD3] about the connectedness of the space of symplectic embeddings of disjoint unions of balls into certain 4-dimensional symplectic manifolds – in particular, into ℂ​P2{\mathbb{C}}P^{2}, ℂ​P1×ℂ​P1{\mathbb{C}}P^{1}\times{\mathbb{C}}P^{1} and their blow-ups (see also [McD1, McD2, Lal, Bir1] for earlier partial results on the subject and
[McD4],
[BLW],
[Cr]
for more recent extensions of McDuff’s result).
In this paper, in an attempt to get more information about the connectedness question for symplectic manifolds of dimension higher than 4, we consider a more restrictive class of
symplectic embeddings of disjoint unions of domains.
Namely, let MM be a
manifold equipped with a
Kähler-type symplectic form ω\omega – i.e., a symplectic form compatible with some (not a priori fixed)
integrable complex structure on MM; such complex structures will be called Kähler-type complex structures.
We will denote by 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M) the space of Kähler-type complex structures on MM.
We consider symplectic embeddings (of disjoint unions of domains) into (M,ω)(M,\omega) that are holomorphic with respect to some (not a priori fixed) integrable (Kähler-type) complex structure II on MM which is compatible with ω\omega, or tamed by it.
We will say that such a symplectic embedding is of Kähler type, or, respectively, tame.
The existence of Kähler-type embeddings, for a fixed isotopy class of the complex structure II on MM compatible with ω\omega, has been previously studied – in a different guise –
in [Ec], [Fl], [LuWa], [Tr], [WN1], [WN2], see 2.3, 3.2 and part 3 of 3.4.
With this terminology, we have the following hierarchy of properties of the embeddings:
Kähler-type⟹tame⟹symplectic.\textrm{K\"{a}hler-type}\ \Longrightarrow\ \textrm{tame}\Longrightarrow\ \textrm{symplectic}.
A Kähler-type embedding of a domain in ℝ2​n{\mathbb{R}}^{2n} (equipped with the standard symplectic and complex structures) into (M,ω)(M,\omega) which is holomorphic with respect to a complex structure II compatible with ω\omega can be equivalently described as a Kähler-isometric, or just Kähler, embedding of the domain into MM equipped with the Kähler metric ω(⋅,I⋅)+−1ω(⋅,⋅)\omega(\cdot,I\cdot)+\sqrt{-1}\omega(\cdot,\cdot).
In other words, an embedding of a domain into (M,ω)(M,\omega) is of Kähler type if and only if it is Kähler with respect to some Kähler metric whose imaginary part is ω\omega. The restriction of the Kähler metric to the image of the embedding is the standard flat metric.
There do exist symplectic embeddings of balls that are not of Kähler type (see below). We do not know whether these
symplectic embeddings are tame or whether there exist tame embeddings (of balls or any other domains) that are not of Kähler-type – see 3.4 and
3.4. Let us note that for a disjoint union of starshaped domains (with a piecewise-smooth boundary)
its Kähler-type and tame
embeddings form Symp⁡(M)\mathrm{Symp}(M)-invariant open subsets of the space of its symplectic embeddings into (M,ω)(M,\omega) (with respect to the C∞C^{\infty}-topology) – see Section 5.1.
As far as the connectedness of the spaces of Kähler-type embeddings is concerned, let us note first that a Kähler embedding into (M,ω)(M,\omega) may be holomorphic with respect to different complex structures compatible with ω\omega, and, second, that two Kähler-type embeddings lying in the same Symp0​(M,ω)\mathrm{Symp}_{0}(M,\omega)-orbit have to be holomorphic with respect to complex structures lying in the same orbit of the Symp0​(M,ω)\mathrm{Symp}_{0}(M,\omega)-action on the space 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M) of Kähler-type complex structures on MM,
hence in the same connected component of the set of complex structures on MM compatible with ω\omega.
Unfortunately, we know almost nothing about the latter connected components or about the space 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)/Symp0​(M,ω)\mathit{CK}(M)/\mathrm{Symp}_{0}(M,\omega). Because of this, we cannot say much about the orbits of the Symp0​(M,ω)\mathrm{Symp}_{0}(M,\omega)-action on the space of Kähler-type embeddings of
given domains into (M,ω)(M,\omega). However, we can say more about the actions of Symp⁡(M,ω)∩Diff0​(M)\mathrm{Symp}(M,\omega)\cap\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M)
and of the symplectic Torelli group SympH​(M,ω)\mathrm{Symp}_{H}(M,\omega) (the group of symplectomorphisms of (M,ω)(M,\omega) acting trivially on the homology)
on the spaces of Kähler-type embeddings of balls into (M,ω)(M,\omega).
Namely, assume that (M,ω)(M,\omega) is either of the following:
ℂ​Pn{\mathbb{C}}P^{n} with the standard symplectic form; 𝕋2​n{\mathbb{T}}^{2n} or a K3 surface equipped with a Kähler-type symplectic form ω\omega which is irrational (i.e., the real cohomology class [ω]∈H2​(M,ℝ)[\omega]\in H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{R}}) is not a real multiple of a rational one). Then
any two
Kähler-type embeddings of an arbitrary disjoint union of balls
into (M,ω)(M,\omega) can be mapped into each other by
the action of SympH​(M,ω)\mathrm{Symp}_{H}(M,\omega). If these embeddings are holomorphic
with respect to complex structures compatible with ω\omega and lying in the same connected component of
𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M), then these embeddings can be mapped into each other by
the action of Symp⁡(M,ω)∩Diff0​(M)\mathrm{Symp}(M,\omega)\cap\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M). In particular, these two Kähler-type embeddings are holomorphic with respect to isotopic
complex structures compatible with ω\omega.
Note that if (M,ω)(M,\omega) is a rational or ruled closed symplectic 4-manifold, or if (M,ω)(M,\omega) is obtained from a rational or ruled closed symplectic 4-manifold by removing either a closed 2-dimensional symplectic submanifold or a Lagrangian submanifold diffeomorphic to 𝕊2{\mathbb{S}}^{2} or ℝ​P2{\mathbb{R}}P^{2} (for instance, if (M,ω)(M,\omega) is the standard symplectic ball), then
any two symplectic
embeddings of an arbitrary disjoint union of closed balls
into (M,ω)(M,\omega) can be mapped into each other by
the action of Symp0c​(M,ω)\mathrm{Symp}^{c}_{0}(M,\omega) – see [McD3], [BLW] (cf. [McD1, McD2, Lal, Bir1] for earlier partial results on the subject).
In particular, this is true for any two Kähler-type, or tame, embeddings and thus the space of such embeddings is connected (see 3.1 below).
Let us now discuss our results on the existence of Kähler-type and tame embeddings.
Following the terminology in [EV1], we say that Kähler-type,
or tame,
embeddings of a disjoint union 𝕎=⨆i=1kWi{\mathbb{W}}=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}W_{i} of compact domains with piecewise boundary into (M,ω)(M,\omega) are unobstructed if there are no obstructions for the existence of such embeddings
of λ​𝕎=⨆i=1kλ​Wi\lambda{\mathbb{W}}=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}\lambda W_{i}, λ>0\lambda>0, into (M,ω)(M,\omega) other than the symplectic volume. (The domains we consider
always lie in the standard symplectic ℝ2​n{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}.) Note that if Kähler-type embeddings of a disjoint union of domains into (M,ω)(M,\omega) are unobstructed, this means, in particular, that one can choose a complex structure II on MM compatible with ω\omega so that the Kähler metric ω(⋅,I⋅)+−1ω(⋅,⋅)\omega(\cdot,I\cdot)+\sqrt{-1}\omega(\cdot,\cdot) on MM is flat outside a set of arbitrarily small volume.
Since the classical work of McDuff-Polterovich [McDP], it has been known that proving the existence of symplectic embeddings of closed balls ⨆i=1kB⁡(ri)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B(r_{i}) of radii r1,…,rkr_{1},\ldots,r_{k} into a closed symplectic manifold (M,ω)(M,\omega) is equivalent to showing that an appropriate cohomology class of the complex blow-up M~{\widetilde{M}} of MM at kk points can be represented by a symplectic form with suitable properties.
In this paper we extend the results of McDuff and Polterovich [McDP] to Kähler-type embeddings of ⨆i=1kB⁡(ri)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B(r_{i}). Namely, assume MM is closed. For each 𝕩=(x1,…,xk){\mathbbm{x}}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}), x1,…,xk∈Mx_{1},\ldots,x_{k}\in M, and each complex structure II on MM, denote by
M~I,𝕩{\widetilde{M}}_{I,{\mathbbm{x}}} the complex blow-up of (M,I)(M,I) at x1,…,xkx_{1},\ldots,x_{k}.
Let I~{\widetilde{I}} be the lift of II to M~{\widetilde{M}}.
We show that ⨆i=1kB⁡(ri)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B(r_{i}) admits a Kähler-type embedding into (M,ω)(M,\omega)
if and only if for some complex structure II compatible with ω\omega and some 𝕩{\mathbbm{x}}
the cohomology class Π∗​[ω]−π​∑i=1kri2​ei∈H2​(M~I,𝕩,ℝ)\Pi^{*}[\omega]-\pi\sum_{i=1}^{k}r_{i}^{2}e_{i}\in H^{2}({\widetilde{M}}_{I,{\mathbbm{x}}};{\mathbb{R}}) is Kähler with respect to I~{\widetilde{I}} (i.e., can be represented by a Kähler form on (M~I,𝕩,I~)({\widetilde{M}}_{I,{\mathbbm{x}}},{\widetilde{I}})). In fact, if this cohomology class is Kähler, then ⨆i=1kB⁡(ri)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B(r_{i}) admits a Kähler-type embedding into (M,ω)(M,\omega) which is holomorphic with respect to a complex structure on MM compatible with ω\omega and smoothly isotopic to II.
Together with information on the Kähler cones of (M~I,𝕩,I~)({\widetilde{M}}_{I,{\mathbbm{x}}},{\widetilde{I}}) for various I~{\widetilde{I}} (compatible with ω\omega) and 𝕩{\mathbbm{x}} this allows us to show the existence of Kähler-type embeddings of ⨆i=1kB⁡(ri)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B(r_{i}) into certain (M,ω)(M,\omega) (see Section 3.1).
Here are some of the applications for particular symplectic manifolds (see Section 3):
If (M,ω)(M,\omega) is either the standard symplectic ℂ​Pn{\mathbb{C}}P^{n} or a standard symplectic ball of real dimension 2​n2n, then for any l∈ℤ>0l\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}, Kähler-type embeddings of a disjoint union of lnl^{n} equal balls are unobstructed. In particular, this implies that for the standard complex structure on ℂ​Pn{\mathbb{C}}P^{n} and
lnl^{n} equal balls of total
symplectic volume smaller than the volume of ℂ​Pn{\mathbb{C}}P^{n}, there exists a Kähler form on ℂ​Pn{\mathbb{C}}P^{n}, isotopic to the standard Fubini-Study form, so that the resulting Kähler manifold admits a Kähler (i.e., both holomorphic and symplectic) embedding of the lnl^{n} equal balls – meaning, in particular, that the Kähler metric is a standard flat metric on the image of the balls.
For M=ℂ​P2M={\mathbb{C}}P^{2} we also describe exactly which disjoint unions of 1≤k≤81\leq k\leq 8 (possibly different) balls admit Kähler-type embeddings into MM.
It then follows from [McD3] that any symplectic embedding of such a disjoint union of balls into ℂ​P2{\mathbb{C}}P^{2} is, in fact, of Kähler type.
In the cases where MM is ℂ​P1×ℂ​P1{\mathbb{C}}P^{1}\times{\mathbb{C}}P^{1} or a complex blow-up of ℂ​P2{\mathbb{C}}P^{2} at one point equipped with
any symplectic form compatible with the orientation, we describe exactly which disjoint unions of 1≤k≤71\leq k\leq 7 (possibly different) balls admit Kähler-type embeddings into MM that are holomorphic with respect to a complex structure isotopic to the “standard” one and compatible with the symplectic form.
It again follows from [McD3] that any symplectic embedding of such a disjoint union of balls into MM is, in fact, of Kähler type.
If MM is either 𝕋2​n{\mathbb{T}}^{2n} or a K3 surface and the Kähler-type symplectic form ω\omega on MM is irrational (i.e., the real cohomology class [ω]∈H2​(M,ℝ)[\omega]\in H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{R}}) is not a real multiple of a rational one), then Kähler-type embeddings of any disjoint union of balls into (M,ω)(M,\omega) are unobstructed. If ω\omega is rational (i.e., [ω]∈H2​(M,ℝ)[\omega]\in H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{R}}) is a real multiple of a rational class),
then
there may be obstructions for the existence of Kähler-type embeddings of disjoint unions of balls into (M,ω)(M,\omega) that are independent of the symplectic volume. For instance, in the case M=𝕋4M={\mathbb{T}}^{4} there are obstructions coming from the Seshadri constants (see 3.4). In particular, this shows that there exist symplectic embeddings of disjoint unions of balls into (𝕋4,ω)({\mathbb{T}}^{4},\omega) that are not Kähler-type.
Finally, we show that tame embeddings of disjoint unions of equal balls, or equal polydisks, or equal parallelepipeds, into (𝕋2​n,ω)({\mathbb{T}}^{2n},\omega)
are unobstructed provided ω\omega lies in a Diff0​(𝕋2​n)\mathrm{Diff}_{0}({\mathbb{T}}^{2n})-invariant open dense set of Kähler-type symplectic forms on 𝕋2​n{\mathbb{T}}^{2n} containing all the irrational forms (the set depends on the embedded domains).
By choosing possibly smaller Diff0​(𝕋2​n)\mathrm{Diff}_{0}({\mathbb{T}}^{2n})-invariant open dense sets of such ω\omega we make sure that the unobstructedness holds, in fact, for the tame embeddings into (𝕋2​n,ω)({\mathbb{T}}^{2n},\omega) which are holomorphic with respect to complex structures that are not only tamed by ω\omega but also “arbitrarily close” (in the sense of Hodge theory) to complex structures compatible with ω\omega.
Kähler-type (not just tame) embeddings of disjoint unions of equal balls, or equal polydisks, or equal parallelepipeds, into (𝕋2​n,ω)({\mathbb{T}}^{2n},\omega) are unobstructed if ω\omega
is an irrational Kähler-type symplectic form lying in an appropriate dense Diff0​(𝕋2​n)\mathrm{Diff}_{0}({\mathbb{T}}^{2n})-orbit in the space of forms (the orbit depends on the embedded domains). Similar results concerning balls and parallelepipeds hold also for symplectic manifolds underlying Kähler K3 surfaces.
We also discuss extensions of the results for K3 surfaces to a larger class of hyperkähler manifolds – see Section 3.5.
2 Preliminaries
Assume that MM is a smooth manifold of real dimension 2​n2n.
Denote by Diff⁡(M)\mathrm{Diff}(M) the group of diffeomorphisms of MM and by Diff0​(M)\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M) its identity component, and by DiffH​(M)\mathrm{Diff}_{H}(M) the subgroup of Diff⁡(M)\mathrm{Diff}(M) formed by the diffeomorphisms acting trivially on the homology of MM.
For a symplectic form ω\omega on MM denote by Symp⁡(M,ω)\mathrm{Symp}(M,\omega) the group of symplectomorphisms of (M,ω)(M,\omega), by Symp0​(M,ω)\mathrm{Symp}_{0}(M,\omega)
its identity component and by SympH​(M,ω)\mathrm{Symp}_{H}(M,\omega) the symplectic Torelli group, i.e. the group of symplectomorphisms of (M,ω)(M,\omega) acting trivially on homology. For a non-compact symplectic manifold (M,ω)(M,\omega) we denote by Sympc​(M,ω)\mathrm{Symp}^{c}(M,\omega)
the group of compactly supported symplectomorphisms of (M,ω)(M,\omega) and by Symp0c​(M,ω)\mathrm{Symp}^{c}_{0}(M,\omega) its identity component.
2.1 Kähler-type complex and symplectic structures
Let ω\omega and II be a symplectic form and a complex structure on MM. The form ω\omega tames II if ω⁡(v,I​v)>0\omega(v,Iv)>0 for any non-zero v∈T​Mv\in TM, and is compatible with II if it tames II and is also II-invariant,
meaning that ω(⋅,I⋅)+iω(⋅,⋅)\omega(\cdot,I\cdot)+i\omega(\cdot,\cdot) is a Hermitian metric on (M,I)(M,I). In the latter case the pair (ω,I)(\omega,I) is called
a Kähler structure on MM.
A cohomology class in H2​(M,ℝ)H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{R}}) is called Kähler (with respect to a complex structure II on MM), if it can be represented by a symplectic form on MM compatible with II. The cohomology classes that are Kähler with respect to II form the Kähler cone of (M,I)(M,I) in H2​(M,ℝ)H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{R}}).
A symplectic/complex structure on MM is said to be of Kähler type, if it appears in some Kähler structure on MM.
Example 2.1
Any complex structure on ℂ​P2{\mathbb{C}}P^{2} is of Kähler type [Yau1]. Whether this holds also for ℂ​Pn{\mathbb{C}}P^{n}, n>2n>2, is
a well-known open question – see e.g. [LiWo, Tos], cf. [HuKP].
For a closed manifold MM, dimℂM=2\dim_{\mathbb{C}}M=2, with an even b2​(M)b_{2}(M), any complex structure on MM is of Kähler type [Buch, Lam]. In particular,
any complex structure on ℂ​P1×ℂ​P1{\mathbb{C}}P^{1}\times{\mathbb{C}}P^{1}, ℂ​P2​♯​ℂ​P2¯{\mathbb{C}}P^{2}\sharp\overline{{\mathbb{C}}P^{2}} and 𝕋4{\mathbb{T}}^{4} is of Kähler type.
The Kähler-type symplectic/complex structures on 𝕋2​n=ℝ2​n/ℤ2​n{\mathbb{T}}^{2n}={\mathbb{R}}^{2n}/{\mathbb{Z}}^{2n} are exactly the ones that can be mapped
by a diffeomorphism of 𝕋2​n{\mathbb{T}}^{2n} to a linear symplectic/complex structure – i.e., a symplectic/complex structure whose lift to ℝ2​n{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}
has constant coefficients
(see e.g. [EV1, Prop. 6.1]).
There do exist complex structures on 𝕋2​n{\mathbb{T}}^{2n}, for each n≥3n\geq 3, that are not of Kähler-type [Som, p.212], [BSV].
If MM is a smooth manifold underlying a complex K3 surface, then any
complex structure on MM is of Kähler-type [Siu] and
its first Chern class is zero (see e.g. [Bea2])11
1
In fact, all smooth manifolds MM underlying a complex K3 surface
are diffeomorphic. They all are compact and connected. All complex structures on such an MM define the
same orientation on MM so that b+​(M)=3b_{+}(M)=3 and
b−​(M)=19b_{-}(M)=19 – see e.g. [Bea2].
Further on, we will always equip a K3 surface with this
standard orientation. Alternatively, to fix the orientation,
one can use the fact that all complex K3 surfaces are deformation equivalent and, in
particular, oriented diffeomorphic – see e.g. [Bea2]..
It is well-known that any complex
structure on such an MM appears in a hyperkähler
structure and so does any Kähler-type symplectic form
on MM – see e.g.
[EV3, Prop. 5.1].
It is a well-known open question whether all symplectic forms on 𝕋2​n{\mathbb{T}}^{2n} and K3 surfaces (equipped with the standard smooth structures) are of Kähler type – see e.g.
[Don]. (There do exist exotic smooth structures on topological K3 surfaces that admit symplectic forms – see [FiS], cf. [Ch]; it follows from the classification of complex surfaces that these exotic smooth K3 surfaces do not admit complex structures and therefore the symplectic forms on them are not of Kähler type). Using the Kodaira-Spencer stability theorem [KoS2] and Torelli theorems for tori (see e.g. [BaHPV, Ch. I, Thm. 14.2]) and K3 surfaces (see [BurR], cf. [Bea2, p.96]) one can show that the Kähler-type forms on these manifolds form an open subset of the space of all symplectic forms (with respect to the C∞C^{\infty}-topology – see e.g. [AmV1], cf. [StT] for a more general result).
Denote by 𝑆𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{SK}(M) the space of all Kähler-type symplectic forms on MM and by 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M) the space of all Kähler-type complex structures on MM.
We equip these spaces with the C∞C^{\infty}-topologies (the complex structures are viewed here as integrable almost-complex structures – i.e., (1,1)(1,1)-tensors).
The space 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M) is an open subspace of the space of all complex structures on MM; both of these spaces are
locally C∞C^{\infty}-path connected – see 13.2.
Given ω∈𝑆𝐾⁡(M)\omega\in\mathit{SK}(M), denote by 𝐶𝑚𝑝𝑡⁡(M,ω)⊂𝐶𝐾⁡(M){\it Cmpt}(M,\omega)\subset\mathit{CK}(M) the space of all (Kähler-type) complex structures on MM compatible with ω\omega.
If 𝒞0{\mathcal{C}}_{0} is a connected component of 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M), we say that ω\omega and
𝒞0{\mathcal{C}}_{0} are compatible, if some complex structure lying in 𝒞0{\mathcal{C}}_{0} is compatible with ω\omega
– i.e., if the set 𝒞0∩𝐶𝑚𝑝𝑡⁡(M,ω){\mathcal{C}}_{0}\cap{\it Cmpt}(M,\omega) is non-empty.
With this terminology, every Kähler-type ω\omega is compatible with some connected component of 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M).
Note that the space 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M) is invariant under the natural action of Diff⁡(M)\mathrm{Diff}(M) (and hence of Diff0​(M)\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M)) on the space of complex structures on MM, but the space
𝐶𝑚𝑝𝑡⁡(M,ω){\it Cmpt}(M,\omega) is not invariant even under the Diff0​(M)\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M)-action.
Definition 2.2
Define the Teichmüller space of Kähler-type complex structures on MM as
𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑⁡(M):=𝐶𝐾⁡(M)/Diff0​(M),{\tt Teich}(M):=\mathit{CK}(M)/\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M),
and if 𝒞0{\mathcal{C}}_{0} is a connected component of 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M), then
𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M):=𝒞0/Diff0​(M).{\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M):={\mathcal{C}}_{0}/\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M).
The set 𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M){\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M) is a connected component of 𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑⁡(M){\tt Teich}(M).
Given I∈𝐶𝐾⁡(M)I\in\mathit{CK}(M), denote by [I]∈𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑⁡(M)[I]\in{\tt Teich}(M) the image of II under the projection 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)→𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M)\to{\tt Teich}(M).
Let ω∈𝑆𝐾⁡(M)\omega\in\mathit{SK}(M). Denote the image of 𝐶𝑚𝑝𝑡⁡(M,ω){\it Cmpt}(M,\omega) under
the quotient projection 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)→𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M)\to{\tt Teich}(M)
by
𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑⁡(M,ω):=Diff0​(M)⋅𝐶𝑚𝑝𝑡⁡(M,ω)Diff0​(M)⊂𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑⁡(M).{\tt Teich}(M,\omega):=\frac{\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M)\cdot{\it Cmpt}(M,\omega)}{\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M)}\subset{\tt Teich}(M).
In other words, 𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑⁡(M,ω){\tt Teich}(M,\omega) is the set of points of 𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑⁡(M){\tt Teich}(M) that can be represented by complex structures
on MM compatible with ω\omega.
Given a connected component 𝒞0{\mathcal{C}}_{0} of 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M), the image of 𝐶𝑚𝑝𝑡⁡(M,ω)∩𝒞0{\it Cmpt}(M,\omega)\cap{\mathcal{C}}_{0} under
the quotient projection 𝒞0→𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M){\mathcal{C}}_{0}\to{\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M)
will be denoted by
𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M,ω):=Diff0​(M)⋅(𝐶𝑚𝑝𝑡⁡(M,ω)∩𝒞0)Diff0​(M)={\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M,\omega):=\frac{\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M)\cdot\left({\it Cmpt}(M,\omega)\cap{\mathcal{C}}_{0}\right)}{\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M)}=
=𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑⁡(M,ω)∩𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M).={\tt Teich}(M,\omega)\cap{\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M).
Note that the space 𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑⁡(M){\tt Teich}(M) may not be Hausdorff – this is the case, for instance, when MM is a smooth manifold underlying a complex K3 surface, see 10.2.
2.2 Hodge decomposition
Assume MM is closed.
A complex structure II on MM defines a (p,q)(p,q)-decomposition of any (complex-valued) differential kk-form η\eta on MM, k=0,1,…,2​nk=0,1,\ldots,2n – we will denote it by η=∑p+q=kηIp,q\eta=\sum_{p+q=k}\eta_{I}^{p,q}.
If II is of Kähler type, then the (p,q)(p,q)-decomposition on differential forms, defined by II, induces a decomposition, called the Hodge decomposition22
2
Although the proof of the
existence of the Hodge decomposition involves both the complex structure II and a Kähler form on (M,I)(M,I), one can show (see e.g. [Voi, Vol.
1, Prop. 6.11]) that, in fact, the Hodge
decomposition on the cohomology depends only on II., on the complex cohomology of MM: Hk(M;ℂ)=⊕p+q=kHIp,q(M;ℂ)H^{k}(M;{\mathbb{C}})=\oplus_{p+q=k}H_{I}^{p,q}(M;{\mathbb{C}}).
Given a cohomology class α∈H∗​(M,ℂ)\alpha\in H^{*}(M;{\mathbb{C}}), we will denote its (p,q)(p,q)-component in the Hodge decomposition by αIp,q∈HIp,q​(M,ℂ)\alpha_{I}^{p,q}\in H_{I}^{p,q}(M;{\mathbb{C}}).
We denote HIp,q​(M,ℝ):=HIp,q​(M,ℂ)∩H∗​(M,ℝ)H^{p,q}_{I}(M;{\mathbb{R}}):=H^{p,q}_{I}(M;{\mathbb{C}})\cap H^{*}(M;{\mathbb{R}}).
For a real cohomology class α∈H2​p​(M,ℝ)\alpha\in H^{2p}(M;{\mathbb{R}}) its (p,p)(p,p)-component is also real.
A differential form on MM is called a (p,q)(p,q)-form, and a cohomology class of MM is called a (p,q)(p,q)-class (with respect to II), if their (p,q)(p,q)-component is their only non-zero component.
If ω\omega is compatible with II, then it is a (1,1)(1,1)-form with respect to II, and, accordingly, its cohomology class is a (1,1)(1,1)-class.
If a cohomology class α∈H2​(M,ℝ)\alpha\in H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{R}}) is Kähler with respect to a Kähler-type complex structure II on MM, then
for any Kähler-type complex structure I′I^{\prime} sufficiently C∞C^{\infty}-close to II the class αI′1,1\alpha_{I^{\prime}}^{1,1} is Kähler with respect to I′I^{\prime} – see 13.2.
2.3 Kähler-type and tame embeddings
Let ω\omega be a Kähler-type symplectic form on MM.
The symplectic and complex structures on ℝ2​n{\mathbb{R}}^{2n} are always assumed to be the standard ones and will be denoted, respectively, by ω0\omega_{0} and J0J_{0}.
Let W1,…,Wk⊂ℝ2​nW_{1},\ldots,W_{k}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{2n} be compact domains with piecewise-smooth boundaries whose interiors contain the origin. Denote
𝕎:=⨆i=1kWi.{\mathbb{W}}:=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}W_{i}.
Given λ>0\lambda>0, define
λ​𝕎:=⨆i=1kλ​Wi.\lambda{\mathbb{W}}:=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}\lambda W_{i}.
We say that f:𝕎→Mf:{\mathbb{W}}\to M is a smooth/symplectic/holomorphic embedding if it extends to such an embedding of the disjoint union of some open neighborhoods of WiW_{i}, i=1,…,ki=1,\ldots,k. We will denote by fi:Wi→Mf_{i}:W_{i}\to M, i=1,…,ki=1,\ldots,k, the restriction of such an extension to
a neighborhood of WiW_{i}.
Clearly, if f:𝕎→(M,ω)f:{\mathbb{W}}\to(M,\omega) is a symplectic embedding, then
Vol⁡(𝕎,ω0)<Vol⁡(M,ω)=⟨[ω]n,[M]⟩.\textstyle{\rm{Vol}\,}({\mathbb{W}},\omega_{0})<{\rm{Vol}\,}(M,\omega)=\langle[\omega]^{n},[M]\rangle.
(Recall that Vol{\rm{Vol}\,} stands for the symplectic volume and ⟨⋅,⋅⟩\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle stands for the natural pairing between cohomology and homology classes.)
Definition 2.3
Let f:𝕎=⨆i=1kWi→(M2​n,ω)f:{\mathbb{W}}=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}W_{i}\to(M^{2n},\omega)
be an embedding.
Given a Kähler-type complex structure I∈𝐶𝑚𝑝𝑡⁡(M,ω)I\in{\it Cmpt}(M,\omega), we say that the embedding ff is of [I][I]-Kähler type, if it is symplectic with respect to ω\omega and holomorphic with respect to some (not a priori fixed) complex structure JJ on MM compatible with ω\omega and isotopic to II, i.e. [J]=[I]∈𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑⁡(M,ω)[J]=[I]\in{\tt Teich}(M,\omega). (Such a JJ is tautologically of Kähler-type).
We say that the embedding ff is of Kähler type, if it is
symplectic with respect to ω\omega and holomorphic with respect to some (not a priori fixed) complex structure compatible with ω\omega –
or, in other words, if ff is of [I][I]-Kähler type for some (not a priori fixed, and possibly non-unique) I∈𝐶𝑚𝑝𝑡⁡(M,ω)I\in{\it Cmpt}(M,\omega).
Given a fixed Kähler structure (ω,I)(\omega,I) on MM, we say that f:𝕎→(M2​n,ω,I)f:{\mathbb{W}}\to(M^{2n},\omega,I) is a Kähler embedding if it is Kähler-isometric – i.e. both symplectic with respect to ω\omega and holomorphic with respect to II.
We say that the embedding f:𝕎→(M2​n,ω)f:{\mathbb{W}}\to(M^{2n},\omega) is tame if it is symplectic with respect to ω\omega and holomorphic with respect to some (not a priori fixed) Kähler-type complex structure II tamed by ω\omega.
Assuming that MM is closed
and ε>0\varepsilon>0, we say that the embedding ff is ε\varepsilon-tame if it is symplectic with respect to ω\omega and holomorphic with respect to some (not a priori fixed) Kähler-type complex structure II so that
•
II is tamed by ω\omega,
•
the class
[ω]I1,1[\omega]_{I}^{1,1} is Kähler,
•
|⟨([ω]I2,0+[ω]I0,2)n,[M]⟩|<ε\left|\left\langle\left([\omega]_{I}^{2,0}+[\omega]_{I}^{0,2}\right)^{n},[M]\right\rangle\right|<\varepsilon.
With the terminology as in 2.3, for an embedding into a (closed) symplectic manifold
(M,ω)(M,\omega)
we have the following equivalences and hierarchy of properties:
Kähler-type ⟺\Longleftrightarrow [I][I]-Kähler type for some (possibly non-unique!) I∈𝐶𝑚𝑝𝑡⁡(M,ω)I\in{\it Cmpt}(M,\omega),
tame ⟺\Longleftrightarrow ε\varepsilon-tame for some ε\varepsilon,
Kähler-type ⟹\Longrightarrow ε\varepsilon-tame for every ε\varepsilon ⟹\Longrightarrow tame ⟹\Longrightarrow symplectic.
In general, there do exist symplectic embeddings of balls that are not of Kähler-type (see 3.4), but we do not know whether there exist
symplectic embeddings of balls or any other domains into any MM, equipped with a Kähler-type symplectic form, that are not tame, or tame embeddings that are not of Kähler-type – see
3.4
and
3.4.
Remark 2.4
For any I∈𝐶𝑚𝑝𝑡⁡(M,ω)I\in{\it Cmpt}(M,\omega) any finite disjoint union of sufficiently small balls (and hence of arbitrary sufficiently small domains in (ℝ2​n,ω0)({\mathbb{R}}^{2n},\omega_{0})) admits an [I][I]-Kähler-type embedding into (M,ω)(M,\omega) – this follows e.g. from [McDP, Prop. 5.5.A]).
Remark 2.5
If a Kähler manifold (M,ω,I)(M,\omega,I) admits a Kähler embedding of a domain in ℝ2​n{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}, its restriction to the image of the embedding is flat. This provides a strong obstruction to the existence of Kähler embeddings (if one fixes a Kähler structure on the manifold).
Remark 2.6
The existence of [I][I]-Kähler-type embeddings has been previously studied – in a different guise – in [Ec], [Fl], [LuWa], [Tr], [WN1], [WN2] in the following setup.
Let (M,ω,I)(M,\omega,I) be a closed Kähler manifold (in [Ec], [Fl], [Tr], [WN1], [WN2] it is assumed that the complex manifold (M,I)(M,I) is projective and the cohomology class [ω]∈H2​(M,ℝ)[\omega]\in H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{R}}) is the first Chern class of an ample line bundle on (M,I)(M,I). Consider the following question: Given a disjoint union 𝕎=⨆i=1kWi{\mathbb{W}}=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}W_{i} of domains in ℝ2​n{\mathbb{R}}^{2n},
does there exist a Kähler form η\eta on (M,I)(M,I), isotopic to ω\omega, such that (M,η,I)(M,\eta,I) admits a Kähler embedding of 𝕎{\mathbb{W}}?
One easily sees that the answer to the latter question is positive if and only if (M,ω)(M,\omega) admits an [I][I]-Kähler-type embedding of 𝕎{\mathbb{W}}.
Let us introduce additional terminology related to Kähler-type
and tame
embeddings.
Let 𝒥⊂𝐶𝐾⁡(M){\mathcal{J}}\subset\mathit{CK}(M) be a non-empty set. If a Kähler-type, or tame,
embedding ff is holomorphic with respect to some complex structure belonging to 𝒥{\mathcal{J}}, we say that ff favors 𝒥{\mathcal{J}}.
In particular, if 𝒥=𝒞0{\mathcal{J}}={\mathcal{C}}_{0} is a connected component 𝒞0{\mathcal{C}}_{0} of 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M), we say that such an ff favors 𝒞0{\mathcal{C}}_{0}, or that 𝒞0{\mathcal{C}}_{0} is a favorite connected component of ff.
With this terminology,
tautologically,
•
For I∈𝐶𝑚𝑝𝑡⁡(M,ω)I\in{\it Cmpt}(M,\omega), every [I][I]-Kähler-type embedding into (M,ω)(M,\omega) favors the Diff0​(M)\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M)-orbit of II in 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M).
•
Every Kähler-type
embedding into (M,ω)(M,\omega) favors a connected component of 𝐶𝑚𝑝𝑡⁡(M,ω){\it Cmpt}(M,\omega), and consequently a connected component of 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M).
Note that, in principle, a Kähler-type embedding may be of [I][I]-Kähler-type for several I∈𝐶𝑚𝑝𝑡⁡(M,ω)I\in{\it Cmpt}(M,\omega) and may favor more than one connected component of 𝐶𝑚𝑝𝑡⁡(M,ω){\it Cmpt}(M,\omega) or of 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M).
The group Symp⁡(M,ω)\mathrm{Symp}(M,\omega) acts naturally on the space of Kähler-type, or tame, or ε\varepsilon-tame,
embeddings 𝕎→(M,ω){\mathbb{W}}\to(M,\omega). Consequently, so do the groups SympH​(M,ω)\mathrm{Symp}_{H}(M,\omega), Symp0​(M,ω)\mathrm{Symp}_{0}(M,\omega) and Symp⁡(M,ω)∩Diff0​(M)\mathrm{Symp}(M,\omega)\cap\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M).
By a standard result concerning extensions of symplectic isotopies (proved as in [McDS, Thms. 3.3.1, 3.3.2]),
if the domains W1,…,WkW_{1},\ldots,W_{k} are starshaped, then
any two symplectic embeddings f:𝕎→(M,ω)f:{\mathbb{W}}\to(M,\omega), f′:𝕎→(M,ω)f^{\prime}:{\mathbb{W}}\to(M,\omega)
can be connected by a (smooth) path of symplectic embeddings 𝕎→(M,ω){\mathbb{W}}\to(M,\omega) if and only if ff and f′f^{\prime}
lie in the same Symp0​(M,ω)\mathrm{Symp}_{0}(M,\omega)-orbit – i.e., there exists ϕ∈Symp0​(M,ω)\phi\in\mathrm{Symp}_{0}(M,\omega) such that ϕ∘f=f′\phi\circ f=f^{\prime}.
Also note, that if ϕ∘f=f′\phi\circ f=f^{\prime} for some ϕ∈Diff⁡(M)\phi\in\mathrm{Diff}(M) and ff is holomorphic with respect to a complex structure II on MM, then f′f^{\prime} is holomorphic with respect to the complex structure ϕ∗​I\phi_{*}I.
These simple observations easily yield the following corollary.
Proposition 2.7
Assume that the domains W1,…,WkW_{1},\ldots,W_{k} are starshaped.
Then any two embeddings f,f′:𝕎=⨆i=1kWi→(M,ω)f,f^{\prime}:{\mathbb{W}}=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}W_{i}\to(M,\omega)
lie in the same connected component of the space of Kähler-type
embeddings 𝕎→(M,ω){\mathbb{W}}\to(M,\omega) if and only if they lie in the
same orbit of the Symp0​(M,ω)\mathrm{Symp}_{0}(M,\omega)-action.
In this case f,f′f,f^{\prime} are holomorphic with respect to complex structures compatible
with ω\omega that lie in the same orbit of the Symp0​(M,ω)\mathrm{Symp}_{0}(M,\omega)-action on 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M). In particular,
such f,f′f,f^{\prime} favor a common connected component of 𝐶𝑚𝑝𝑡⁡(M,ω){\it Cmpt}(M,\omega)
and, consequently, a common connected component of 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M).
If Kähler-type
embeddings f,f′:𝕎→(M,ω)f,f^{\prime}:{\mathbb{W}}\to(M,\omega) lie in the same Symp⁡(M,ω)∩Diff0​(M)\mathrm{Symp}(M,\omega)\cap\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M)-orbit, then they are holomorphic with respect to complex structures compatible
with ω\omega that lie in the same orbit of the Diff0​(M)\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M)-action on 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M). In particular, there exists [I]∈𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑⁡(M,ω)[I]\in{\tt Teich}(M,\omega)
such that both ff and f′f^{\prime} are of [I][I]-Kähler-type, and f,f′f,f^{\prime} favor a common connected component of 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M).
Let us define a quantity that will be important for the study of the portion of the volume of a target closed symplectic manifold that can be filled out by Kähler-type and tame embeddings.
Definition 2.8
Assume MM is a closed manifold equipped with a Kähler-type symplectic form ω\omega.
Define the number 0<νK​(M,ω,𝕎)≤10<\nu_{K}(M,\omega,{\mathbb{W}})\leq 1 by
νK​(M,ω,𝕎):=supλVol⁡(λ​𝕎,ω0)Vol⁡(M,ω),\nu_{K}(M,\omega,{\mathbb{W}}):=\sup_{\lambda}\frac{{\rm{Vol}\,}(\lambda{\mathbb{W}},\omega_{0})}{{\rm{Vol}\,}(M,\omega)},
where the supremum is taken over all λ>0\lambda>0 such that λ​𝕎\lambda{\mathbb{W}} admits a Kähler-type embedding into (M,ω)(M,\omega).
Given
ε>0\varepsilon>0, define the number 0<νT,ε​(M,ω,𝕎)≤10<\nu_{T,\varepsilon}(M,\omega,{\mathbb{W}})\leq 1 by
νT,ε​(M,ω,𝕎):=supλVol⁡(λ​𝕎,ω0)Vol⁡(M,ω),\nu_{T,\varepsilon}(M,\omega,{\mathbb{W}}):=\sup_{\lambda}\frac{{\rm{Vol}\,}(\lambda{\mathbb{W}},\omega_{0})}{{\rm{Vol}\,}(M,\omega)},
where the supremum is taken over all λ>0\lambda>0 such that λ​𝕎\lambda{\mathbb{W}} admits an ε\varepsilon-tame embedding into (M,ω)(M,\omega).
(In view of 2.3, the set of such λ\lambda is always non-empty.)
Remark 2.9
1. Clearly,
νK​(M,ω,𝕎)≤νT,ε1​(M,ω,𝕎)≤νT,ε2​(M,ω,𝕎)\nu_{K}(M,\omega,{\mathbb{W}})\leq\nu_{T,\varepsilon_{1}}(M,\omega,{\mathbb{W}})\leq\nu_{T,\varepsilon_{2}}(M,\omega,{\mathbb{W}})
for any 0<ε1≤ε20<\varepsilon_{1}\leq\varepsilon_{2}.
2. The equality νK​(M,ω,𝕎)=1\nu_{K}(M,\omega,{\mathbb{W}})=1, respectively νT,ε​(M,ω,𝕎)=1\nu_{T,\varepsilon}(M,\omega,{\mathbb{W}})=1, is equivalent
to the claim that the Kähler-type, respectively ε\varepsilon-tame, embeddings of λ​𝕎\lambda{\mathbb{W}} into (M,ω)(M,\omega), for all λ>0\lambda>0, are unobstructed.
3 Main results
In this section we state the main results of the paper.
For r>0r>0 we denote by B2​n​(r)B^{2n}(r) the closed Euclidean 2​n2n-dimensional ball of radius rr in ℝ2​n{\mathbb{R}}^{2n} centered at the origin.
3.1 Kähler-type embeddings of balls in general symplectic manifolds
and Kähler classes on complex blow-ups
In this section MM, dimℝ=2n\dim_{\mathbb{R}}=2n, is a closed connected manifold equipped with a Kähler-type symplectic form ω\omega.
Let k∈ℤ>0k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}, r¯=(r1,…,rk)∈(ℝ>0)k{\bar{r}}=(r_{1},\ldots,r_{k})\in({\mathbb{R}}_{>0})^{k}.
Denote by M^k⊂Mk{\hat{M}}^{k}\subset M^{k} the set of kk-tuples of pairwise distinct points of MM.
For each 𝕩:=(x1,…,xk)∈M^k{\mathbbm{x}}:=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})\in{\hat{M}}^{k}
and each complex structure II on MM, let M~I,𝕩{\widetilde{M}}_{I,{\mathbbm{x}}}
denote the complex blow-up of (M,I)(M,I) at x1,…,xkx_{1},\ldots,x_{k}.
For any II and 𝕩{\mathbbm{x}} we will use the same notation for the following objects (suppressing the dependence on II and 𝕩{\mathbbm{x}}):
- The lift of II to M~I,𝕩{\widetilde{M}}_{I,{\mathbbm{x}}} will be denoted by I~{\widetilde{I}}.
- Π:M~I,𝕩→M\Pi:{\widetilde{M}}_{I,{\mathbbm{x}}}\to M will denote the natural projection.
- The cohomology classes Poincaré-dual to the fundamental homology classes of the exceptional divisors will be denoted by e1,…,ek∈H2​(M~I,𝕩,ℝ)e_{1},\ldots,e_{k}\in H^{2}({\widetilde{M}}_{I,{\mathbbm{x}}};{\mathbb{R}}).
For all II and 𝕩{\mathbbm{x}} the groups H2​(M~I,𝕩,ℝ)H^{2}({\widetilde{M}}_{I,{\mathbbm{x}}};{\mathbb{R}}) can be canonically identified (preserving the numbering of the classes e1,…,ene_{1},\ldots,e_{n}) and we will use these identifications without further mention.
Definition 3.1
Define the set
𝒦⁡(r¯)⊂𝐶𝐾⁡(M)×M^k{\mathcal{K}}({\bar{r}})\subset\mathit{CK}(M)\times{\hat{M}}^{k} as
𝒦(r¯):={(I,𝕩)|I∈Diff0(M)⋅𝐶𝑚𝑝𝑡(M,ω),𝕩∈M^kand{\mathcal{K}}({\bar{r}}):=\Big\{\ (I,{\mathbbm{x}})\ \big|\ I\in\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M)\cdot{\it Cmpt}(M,\omega),{\mathbbm{x}}\in{\hat{M}}^{k}\ \textit{and}
Π∗[ω]−π∑i=1kri2ei∈H2(M~I,𝕩;ℝ)is Kähler with respect toI~},\Pi^{*}[\omega]-\pi\sum_{i=1}^{k}r_{i}^{2}e_{i}\in H^{2}({\widetilde{M}}_{I,{\mathbbm{x}}};{\mathbb{R}})\ \textit{is K\"{a}hler with respect to}\ {\widetilde{I}}\ \Big\},
Let pr:𝒦⁡(r¯)→𝐶𝐾⁡(M){\textit{pr}\,}:{\mathcal{K}}({\bar{r}})\to\mathit{CK}(M) be the natural projection.
Define the set 𝙺⁡(r¯)⊂𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑⁡(M,ω){\tt{K}}({\bar{r}})\subset{\tt Teich}(M,\omega) as
𝙺⁡(r¯):=pr​(𝒦⁡(r¯))/Diff0​(M)={\tt{K}}({\bar{r}}):={\textit{pr}\,}({\mathcal{K}}({\bar{r}}))/\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M)=
={ι∈𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑(M,ω)|∃I∈𝐶𝑚𝑝𝑡(M,ω),[I]=ι,and𝕩∈M^ks.t.=\Big\{\ \iota\in{\tt Teich}(M,\omega)\ \big|\ \exists I\in{\it Cmpt}(M,\omega),[I]=\iota,\ \textit{and}\ {\mathbbm{x}}\in{\hat{M}}^{k}\ \textit{s.t.}
Π∗[ω]−π∑i=1kri2ei∈H2(M~I,𝕩;ℝ)is Kähler with respect toI~}.\Pi^{*}[\omega]-\pi\sum_{i=1}^{k}r_{i}^{2}e_{i}\in H^{2}({\widetilde{M}}_{I,{\mathbbm{x}}};{\mathbb{R}})\ \textit{is K\"{a}hler with respect to}\ {\widetilde{I}}\ \Big\}.
Let 𝒞0{\mathcal{C}}_{0} be a connected component of 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M).
Define the set
𝒦𝒞0​(r¯)⊂𝒞0×M^k{\mathcal{K}}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}({\bar{r}})\subset{\mathcal{C}}_{0}\times{\hat{M}}^{k} as
𝒦𝒞0​(r¯):=(𝒞0×M^k)∩𝒦⁡(r¯)={\mathcal{K}}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}({\bar{r}}):=({\mathcal{C}}_{0}\times{\hat{M}}^{k})\cap{\mathcal{K}}({\bar{r}})=
={(I,𝕩)|I∈Diff0(M)⋅(𝒞0∩𝐶𝑚𝑝𝑡(M,ω)),𝕩∈M^kand=\Big\{\ (I,{\mathbbm{x}})\ \big|\ I\in\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M)\cdot\big({\mathcal{C}}_{0}\cap{\it Cmpt}(M,\omega)\big),{\mathbbm{x}}\in{\hat{M}}^{k}\ \textit{and}
Π∗[ω]−π∑i=1kri2ei∈H2(M~I,𝕩;ℝ)is Kähler with respect toI~}.\Pi^{*}[\omega]-\pi\sum_{i=1}^{k}r_{i}^{2}e_{i}\in H^{2}({\widetilde{M}}_{I,{\mathbbm{x}}};{\mathbb{R}})\ \textit{is K\"{a}hler with respect to}\ {\widetilde{I}}\ \Big\}.
Define the set 𝙺𝒞0​(r¯)⊂𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M,ω){\tt{K}}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}({\bar{r}})\subset{\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M,\omega) as
𝙺𝒞0​(r¯):=𝙺⁡(r¯)∩𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M)=pr​(𝒦𝒞0​(r¯))/Diff0​(M)={\tt{K}}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}({\bar{r}}):={\tt{K}}({\bar{r}})\cap{\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M)={\textit{pr}\,}\left({\mathcal{K}}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}({\bar{r}})\right)/\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M)=
={ι∈𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0(M,ω)|∃I∈𝒞0∩𝐶𝑚𝑝𝑡(M,ω),[I]=ι,and𝕩∈M^ks.t.=\Big\{\ \iota\in{\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M,\omega)\ \big|\ \exists I\in{\mathcal{C}}_{0}\cap{\it Cmpt}(M,\omega),[I]=\iota,\ \textit{and}\ {\mathbbm{x}}\in{\hat{M}}^{k}\ \textit{s.t.}
Π∗[ω]−π∑i=1kri2ei∈H2(M~I,𝕩;ℝ)is Kähler with respect toI~}.\Pi^{*}[\omega]-\pi\sum_{i=1}^{k}r_{i}^{2}e_{i}\in H^{2}({\widetilde{M}}_{I,{\mathbbm{x}}};{\mathbb{R}})\ \textit{is K\"{a}hler with respect to}\ {\widetilde{I}}\ \Big\}.
As we will see in 5.6, the fibers of pr:𝒦⁡(r¯)→𝐶𝐾⁡(M){\textit{pr}\,}:{\mathcal{K}}({\bar{r}})\to\mathit{CK}(M) are connected and therefore 𝙺⁡(r¯){\tt{K}}({\bar{r}}) (respectively, 𝙺𝒞0​(r¯){\tt{K}}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}({\bar{r}}))
is connected if and only if so is 𝒦⁡(r¯){\mathcal{K}}({\bar{r}}) (respectively, 𝒦𝒞0​(r¯){\mathcal{K}}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}({\bar{r}})).
Theorem 3.2
Let MM be a closed manifold equipped with a Kähler-type symplectic form ω\omega.
(I)
The set ⨆i=1kB2​n​(ri)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{2n}(r_{i}) admits a Kähler-type embedding into (M,ω)(M,\omega) if and only if
for some I∈𝐶𝑚𝑝𝑡⁡(M,ω)I\in{\it Cmpt}(M,\omega) and some 𝕩∈M^k{\mathbbm{x}}\in{\hat{M}}^{k} the cohomology class Π∗​[ω]−π​∑i=1kri2​ei∈H2​(M~I,𝕩,ℝ)\Pi^{*}[\omega]-\pi\sum_{i=1}^{k}r_{i}^{2}e_{i}\in H^{2}({\widetilde{M}}_{I,{\mathbbm{x}}};{\mathbb{R}}) is Kähler with respect to I~{\widetilde{I}}.
More precisely, assume II is a complex structure on MM compatible with ω\omega and Σ⊂(M,I)\Sigma\subset(M,I) is a proper (possibly empty) complex submanifold. If M=ℂ​Pn×ℂ​P1M={\mathbb{C}}P^{n}\times{\mathbb{C}}P^{1} (with the product complex structure), we also allow
Σ=(ℂ​Pn−1×ℂ​P1)∪(ℂ​Pn×pt)\Sigma=({\mathbb{C}}P^{n-1}\times{\mathbb{C}}P^{1})\cup({\mathbb{C}}P^{n}\times\textrm{pt}) (which is not smooth).
Then the following conditions are equivalent:
•
There exists 𝕩=(x1,…,xk)∈M^k{\mathbbm{x}}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})\in{\hat{M}}^{k}, x1,…,xk∈M∖Σx_{1},\ldots,x_{k}\in M\setminus\Sigma, such that the cohomology class Π∗​[ω]−π​∑i=1kri2​ei∈H2​(M~I,𝕩,ℝ)\Pi^{*}[\omega]-\pi\sum_{i=1}^{k}r_{i}^{2}e_{i}\in H^{2}({\widetilde{M}}_{I,{\mathbbm{x}}};{\mathbb{R}}) is Kähler with respect to I~{\widetilde{I}}.
•
There exists an [I][I]-Kähler-type embedding f:⨆i=1kB2​n​(ri)→(M∖Σ,ω)f:\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{2n}(r_{i})\to(M\setminus\Sigma,\omega)
which is holomorphic with respect to the complex structure ϕ1∗​I\phi_{1}^{*}I compatible with ω\omega, where
{ϕt}0≤t≤1⊂Diff0​(M)\{\phi_{t}\}_{0\leq t\leq 1}\subset\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M), ϕ0=I​d\phi_{0}=Id, is an isotopy such that ϕt​(Σ)=Σ\phi_{t}(\Sigma)=\Sigma for all t∈[0,1]t\in[0,1].
(II)
Let 𝒞0{\mathcal{C}}_{0} be a connected component of 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M).
Assume that
the set 𝙺𝒞0​(r¯){\tt{K}}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}({\bar{r}}) (or, equivalently, the set 𝒦𝒞0​(r¯){\mathcal{K}}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}({\bar{r}}))
is connected.
Then any two Kähler-type embeddings ⨆i=1kB2​n​(ri)→(M,ω)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{2n}(r_{i})\to(M,\omega) favoring 𝒞0{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
lie in the same Symp⁡(M,ω)∩Diff0​(M)\mathrm{Symp}(M,\omega)\cap\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M)-orbit. In particular, there exists [I]∈𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M,ω)[I]\in{\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M,\omega) such that
both embeddings are of [I][I]-Kähler type.
If, in addition, SympH​(M)\mathrm{Symp}_{H}(M) acts transitively on the set of connected components of 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M) compatible with ω\omega, then
any two Kähler-type embeddings ⨆i=1kB2​n​(ri)→(M,ω)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{2n}(r_{i})\to(M,\omega)
lie in the same SympH​(M,ω)\mathrm{Symp}_{H}(M,\omega)-orbit.
For the proof of 3.1 see Section 5 (5.2).
Remark 3.3
1. For an analogue of part (I) of 3.1 for tame embeddings
see 5.5 below.
2. [Fl, Remark 5.2] states that the methods of [Fl] can be used to prove a result that, in view of
2.3,
is equivalent to Part (I) 3.1 in the case where
ω\omega is compatible with a complex structure II on MM, so that the complex manifold (M,I)(M,I) is projective and
the cohomology class [ω]∈H2​(M,ℝ)[\omega]\in H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{R}}) is the first Chern class of an ample line bundle on (M,I)(M,I).
Recall that the symplectic blow-up construction (see e.g. [McDS, Sec. 7], cf. Section 5.5 below) associates to a complex blow-up of a symplectic manifold at a point (with respect to, say, an almost complex structure compatible with the symplectic form and integrable near the point) a class of symplectic forms on the blow-up. We will call such a symplectic form on the blow-up a blow-up symplectic form. A rational (4-dimensional) symplectic manifold is either ℂ​P2{\mathbb{C}}P^{2} with a standard Fubini-Study symplectic form, or ℂ​P1×ℂ​P1{\mathbb{C}}P^{1}\times{\mathbb{C}}P^{1} with a product symplectic form, or their blow-up at several points equipped with a blow-up
symplectic form.
A ruled (4-dimensional) symplectic manifold is a closed symplectic 4-manifold which is the total space of an 𝕊2{\mathbb{S}}^{2}-fiber bundle over a closed connected orientable 2-dimensional surface, so that all the fibers are symplectic submanifolds [LalM].
For rational or ruled closed symplectic manifolds of real dimension 44 we have a stronger connectedness result, which follows directly
from similar connectedness results for symplectic (not necessarily Kähler-type) embeddings of balls
that were proved in [McD3] and [BLW]
(for previous partial results see [McD1, McD2, Lal, Bir1]).
Theorem 3.4
Let (M,ω)(M,\omega) be a rational or ruled closed symplectic manifold, dimℝM=4\dim_{\mathbb{R}}M=4. Let Σ⊂(M,ω)\Sigma\subset(M,\omega)
be either of the following:
(1) the empty set;
(2) a finite union of closed compact symplectic submanifolds (without boundary) of real dimension 2
whose pairwise intersections (if they exist) are transverse and ω\omega–orthogonal;
(3) a Lagrangian submanifold which is diffeomorphic to either 𝕊2{\mathbb{S}}^{2} or ℝ​P2{\mathbb{R}}P^{2}.
Then for any k∈ℤ>0k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0} and any r1,…,rk>0r_{1},\ldots,r_{k}>0,
the following claims hold:
(I)
Any two Kähler-type, respectively tame, embeddings
⨆i=1kB4​(ri)→(M∖Σ,ω)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{4}(r_{i})\to(M\setminus\Sigma,\omega) lie in the same orbit of the Symp0c​(M∖Σ,ω)\mathrm{Symp}^{c}_{0}(M\setminus\Sigma,\omega)-action.
In particular, (for fixed r1,…,rkr_{1},\ldots,r_{k}) the space of all such Kähler-type, respectively tame, embeddings is connected.
(II) If there exists an [I][I]-Kähler-type, respectively tame, embedding ⨆i=1kB4​(ri)→(M∖Σ,ω)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{4}(r_{i})\to(M\setminus\Sigma,\omega),
then any symplectic embedding ⨆i=1kB4​(ri)→(M∖Σ,ω)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{4}(r_{i})\to(M\setminus\Sigma,\omega) is of [I][I]-Kähler type, respectively tame.
Proof of 3.1:
Any two symplectic embeddings ⨆i=1kB2​n​(ri)→(M∖Σ,ω)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{2n}(r_{i})\to(M\setminus\Sigma,\omega)
lie in the same orbit of the Symp0c​(M∖Σ,ω)\mathrm{Symp}^{c}_{0}(M\setminus\Sigma,\omega)-action:
- In case (1) this was proved in [McD3, Cor. 1.5].
- In case (2), where Σ⊂(M,ω)\Sigma\subset(M,\omega) is just a symplectic submanifold, this was essentially proved in [McD3, Cor. 1.5] – see [BLW, Prop. 2.1] where more details of the proof are spelled out in the case where the symplectic submanifold Σ⊂(M,ω)\Sigma\subset(M,\omega) is a sphere; in fact [McDO, Cor. 1.2.13], used in the proof of [BLW, Prop. 2.1], yields the same claim for any Σ\Sigma as in (2).
- In case (3), this is proved in [BLW, Thm. 1.1].
Since [I][I]-Kähler-type, respectively tame, embeddings into (M∖Σ,ω)(M\setminus\Sigma,\omega) are mapped by
the Symp0c​(M∖Σ,ω)\mathrm{Symp}^{c}_{0}(M\setminus\Sigma,\omega)-action
into [I][I]-Kähler-type, respectively tame, embeddings, we get the
results of both parts of the theorem.
Now let us discuss applications of 3.1
and 3.1 to particular symplectic manifolds.
3.2 The case of projective spaces and their products
Denote by ωF​S\omega_{FS}, or by ωF​S,n\omega_{FS,n}, the Fubini-Study form on ℂ​Pn{\mathbb{C}}P^{n} normalized so that ∫ℂ​P1ωF​S=π\int_{{\mathbb{C}}P^{1}}\omega_{FS}=\pi.
Let Is​tI_{st} be the standard complex structure on ℂ​Pn{\mathbb{C}}P^{n}.
We also set ℂ​P0:=pt{\mathbb{C}}P^{0}:=\textrm{pt}.
Theorem 3.5
Consider the manifold M:=ℂ​Pn1×…×ℂ​PnmM:={\mathbb{C}}P^{n_{1}}\times\ldots\times{\mathbb{C}}P^{n_{m}}, n1,…,nm>0n_{1},\ldots,n_{m}>0, n1+…+nm=:nn_{1}+\ldots+n_{m}=:n, endowed with the symplectic form ω𝕔:=c1​ωF​S,n1⊕…⊕cm​ωF​S,nm\omega_{\mathbbm{c}}:=c_{1}\omega_{FS,n_{1}}\oplus\ldots\oplus c_{m}\omega_{FS,n_{m}}, 𝕔:=(c1,…,cm)∈(ℝ>0)m{\mathbbm{c}}:=(c_{1},\ldots,c_{m})\in({\mathbb{R}}_{>0})^{m}. Let II be the complex
structure on MM which is the product of the standard complex structures on the factors.
Let l1,…,lm∈ℤ>0l_{1},\ldots,l_{m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0} so that [l1:…:lm]=[c1:…:cm]∈ℝPm[l_{1}:\ldots:l_{m}]=[c_{1}:\ldots:c_{m}]\in{\mathbb{R}}P^{m}.
Let
k:=(n1+…+nm)!n1!⋅…⋅nm!​l1n1⋅…⋅lmnm.k:=\frac{(n_{1}+\ldots+n_{m})!}{n_{1}!\cdot\ldots\cdot n_{m}!}l_{1}^{n_{1}}\cdot\ldots\cdot l_{m}^{n_{m}}.
Let Σ⊂(M,I)\Sigma\subset(M,I) be a proper (possibly empty) complex submanifold. If M=ℂ​Pn1×ℂ​P1M={\mathbb{C}}P^{n_{1}}\times{\mathbb{C}}P^{1}, we also allow
Σ=(ℂ​Pn1−1×ℂ​P1)∪(ℂ​Pn1×pt)\Sigma=({\mathbb{C}}P^{n_{1}-1}\times{\mathbb{C}}P^{1})\cup({\mathbb{C}}P^{n_{1}}\times\textrm{pt}).
Then Kähler-type embeddings of disjoint unions of kk equal balls into (M∖Σ,ω𝕔)(M\setminus\Sigma,\omega_{\mathbbm{c}})
are unobstructed.
More precisely, if k​Vol​(B2​n​(r),ω0)<Vol⁡(M,ω)k{\rm{Vol}\,}(B^{2n}(r),\omega_{0})<{\rm{Vol}\,}(M,\omega), then for each proper (possibly empty) complex submanifold Σ⊂(M,I)\Sigma\subset(M,I), there exists an [I][I]-Kähler-type embedding of ⨆i=1kB2​n​(r)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{2n}(r)
into (M,ω)(M,\omega) which is holomorphic with respect to a complex structure on MM that is compatible with ω\omega
and isotopic to II by an isotopy preserving Σ\Sigma (as a set).
For the proof of
3.2 see Section 6 (6).
For n1=…=nm=1n_{1}=\ldots=n_{m}=1, l1=…=lm=ll_{1}=\ldots=l_{m}=l, c1=…=cm=1c_{1}=\ldots=c_{m}=1 and Σ=∅\Sigma=\emptyset,
3.2 yields the following corollary.
Corollary 3.6
Let M:=(ℂ​P1)mM:=({\mathbb{C}}P^{1})^{m}. Consider a symplectic form ω\omega on MM which is the product of equal Fubini-Study forms on the ℂ​P1{\mathbb{C}}P^{1}-factors of MM. are unobstructed.

into (M,ω)(M,\omega) which is holomorphic with respect to a complex structure on MM that is compatible with ω\omega
and isotopic to II.
Now let us consider the case of a single complex projective space.
Theorem 3.7
A. For each l∈ℤ>0l\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0},

into (ℂ​Pn,ωF​S)({\mathbb{C}}P^{n},\omega_{FS}) which is holomorphic with respect to a complex structure on ℂ​Pn{\mathbb{C}}P^{n} that is compatible with ωF​S\omega_{FS}
and isotopic to JJ by an isotopy preserving Σ\Sigma (as a set).
B. The group SympH​(ℂ​Pn,ωF​S)\mathrm{Symp}_{H}({\mathbb{C}}P^{n},\omega_{FS}) acts transitively on the set of connected components of 𝐶𝐾⁡(ℂ​Pn)\mathit{CK}({\mathbb{C}}P^{n}) compatible with
ωF​S\omega_{FS}.
C.
For any k∈ℤ>0k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0} and r1,…,rk>0r_{1},\ldots,r_{k}>0, any two Kähler-type embeddings
⨆i=1kB2​n​(ri)→(ℂ​Pn,ωF​S)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{2n}(r_{i})\to({\mathbb{C}}P^{n},\omega_{FS})
(if they exist!) lie in the same orbit of the SympH​(ℂ​Pn,ωF​S)\mathrm{Symp}_{H}({\mathbb{C}}P^{n},\omega_{FS})-action.
They lie in the same orbit of the Symp⁡(ℂ​Pn,ωF​S)∩Diff0​(ℂ​Pn)\mathrm{Symp}({\mathbb{C}}P^{n},\omega_{FS})\cap\mathrm{Diff}_{0}({\mathbb{C}}P^{n})-action
if and only if they favor a common connected component of 𝐶𝐾⁡(ℂ​Pn)\mathit{CK}({\mathbb{C}}P^{n}). In the latter case there exists [I]∈𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑⁡(ℂ​Pn,ωF​S)[I]\in{\tt Teich}({\mathbb{C}}P^{n},\omega_{FS}) such that
both embeddings are of [I][I]-Kähler type.
Part A of 3.2
follows from
3.2.
For the proof of parts B and C of 3.2 see Section 6 (6).
Remark 3.8
Let us say that a Kähler-type complex structure II on a closed manifold MM
is rigid, if the connected component of 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M) containing II coincides with the Diff0​(M)\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M)-orbit of II
(or, in other words, if any Kähler-type complex structure on MM obtained from II by a smooth deformation is, in fact, isotopic to II).
As we will see in
6, any
Kähler-type complex structure on ℂ​Pn{\mathbb{C}}P^{n} is rigid. More
examples of rigid complex structures include
so-called 𝒵{\mathcal{Z}}-manifolds
(i.e., Calabi-Yau manifolds with
hn−1,1=0h^{n-1,1}=0, [CDP]).
The proof of part C of 3.2 allows to generalize the last claim of part C as follows:
Assume (M,ω)(M,\omega) is a closed manifold such that ω\omega is compatible with a rigid complex structure II. Let 𝒞0{\mathcal{C}}_{0} be a connected component
of 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M) containing II. Then two Kähler-type embeddings ⨆i=1kB2​n​(ri)→(M,ω)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{2n}(r_{i})\to(M,\omega) favoring 𝒞0{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
lie in the same orbit of the Symp⁡(M,ω)∩Diff0​(M)\mathrm{Symp}(M,\omega)\cap\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M)-action.
Applying part A of 3.2 with
Σ=∅\Sigma=\emptyset, with Is​t=JI_{st}=J being fixed, and with ωF​S\omega_{FS} being varied by an isotopy, we get the following immediate corollary.
Corollary 3.9
Let l∈ℤ>0l\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}.
Then for any r>0r>0 such that ln​Vol​(B2​n​(r),ω0)<Vol⁡(ℂ​Pn,ωF​S)l^{n}{\rm{Vol}\,}(B^{2n}(r),\omega_{0})<{\rm{Vol}\,}({\mathbb{C}}P^{n},\omega_{FS}) there exists a Kähler form ω\omega on (ℂ​Pn,Is​t)({\mathbb{C}}P^{n},I_{st}) isotopic to ωF​S\omega_{FS} and such that the Kähler manifold (ℂ​Pn,Is​t,ω)({\mathbb{C}}P^{n},I_{st},\omega) admits a Kähler (i.e., both holomorphic and symplectic) embedding of ⨆i=1lnB2​n​(r)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{l^{n}}B^{2n}(r) with the standard flat Kähler metric on it.
In particular, part A of 3.2 and 3.2
mean that by changing either Is​tI_{st} or ωF​S\omega_{FS} by an isotopy one can get a Kähler metric on ℂ​Pn{\mathbb{C}}P^{n} which is the standard flat Kähler metric on lnl^{n} disjoint balls in ℂ​Pn{\mathbb{C}}P^{n} that fill out an arbitrarily large portion of the volume of the manifold.

For l=1l=1 and n=2n=2 3.2 was proved in [Ec].

For any l∈ℤ>0l\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0} and any r>0r>0 such that ln​Vol​(B2​n​(r),ω0)<Vol⁡(B2​n​(1),ω0)l^{n}{\rm{Vol}\,}(B^{2n}(r),\omega_{0})<{\rm{Vol}\,}(B^{2n}(1),\omega_{0}) there exists a Kähler-type

Now let us focus on the case of (ℂ​P2,ωF​S)({\mathbb{C}}P^{2},\omega_{FS}).
Proposition 3.12
There is only one connected component of 𝐶𝐾⁡(ℂ​P2)\mathit{CK}({\mathbb{C}}P^{2}) compatible with ωF​S\omega_{FS} – it is the connected component
of 𝐶𝐾⁡(ℂ​P2)\mathit{CK}({\mathbb{C}}P^{2}) containing Is​tI_{st}. Any two complex structures in that connected component are isotopic.
For the proof of 3.2 see Section 6 (6).
We do not know whether 𝐶𝐾⁡(ℂ​P2)\mathit{CK}({\mathbb{C}}P^{2}) has more than one connected component – see 6.
3.2 yields the following immediate corollary.
Corollary 3.13
Any Kähler-type embedding (of any disjoint union of domains) into (ℂ​P2,ωF​S)({\mathbb{C}}P^{2},\omega_{FS}) is of [Is​t][I_{st}]-Kähler type: it is symplectic with respect to ωF​S\omega_{FS} and holomorphic with respect to a complex structure on ℂ​P2{\mathbb{C}}P^{2} isotopic to Is​tI_{st} and compatible with ωF​S\omega_{FS}.
Recall the following theorem of McDuff-Polterovich [McDP].
Theorem 3.14
Let Σ⊂(ℂ​P2,Is​t)\Sigma\subset({\mathbb{C}}P^{2},I_{st}) be a proper (possibly empty) complex submanifold.
A. Assume that 1≤k≤81\leq k\leq 8 and r1≥r2≥…≥rk>0r_{1}\geq r_{2}\geq\ldots\geq r_{k}>0.
Then
the following conditions are equivalent:
•
There exists a symplectic embedding ⨆i=1kB4​(ri)→(ℂ​P2∖Σ,ωF​S)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{4}(r_{i})\to({\mathbb{C}}P^{2}\setminus\Sigma,\omega_{FS}).
•
The radii r1,…,rkr_{1},\ldots,r_{k} satisfy the following inequalities (listed in [McDP, Cor. 1.3G]):
(v) ∑i=1kri4<1\sum_{i=1}^{k}r_{i}^{4}<1 (volume inequality),
(c1) r12+r22<1r_{1}^{2}+r_{2}^{2}<1, if 2≤k≤82\leq k\leq 8,
(c2) r12+…+r52<2r_{1}^{2}+\ldots+r_{5}^{2}<2, if 5≤k≤85\leq k\leq 8,
(c3) 2​r12+∑i=27ri2<32r_{1}^{2}+\sum_{i=2}^{7}r_{i}^{2}<3, if 7≤k≤87\leq k\leq 8,
(c4) 2​r12+2​r22+2​r32+r42+…+r82<42r_{1}^{2}+2r_{2}^{2}+2r_{3}^{2}+r_{4}^{2}+\ldots+r_{8}^{2}<4, if k=8k=8,
(c5) 2​∑i=16ri2+r72+r82<52\sum_{i=1}^{6}r_{i}^{2}+r_{7}^{2}+r_{8}^{2}<5, if k=8k=8,
(c6) 3​r12+2​∑i=28ri2<63r_{1}^{2}+2\sum_{i=2}^{8}r_{i}^{2}<6, if k=8k=8.
B. Symplectic embeddings of a disjoint union of k=l2k=l^{2}, l∈ℤ>0l\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}, equal balls into (ℂ​P2∖Σ,ωF​S)({\mathbb{C}}P^{2}\setminus\Sigma,\omega_{FS}) are unobstructed:
if k​Vol​(B4​(r),ω0)<Vol⁡(ℂ​P2,ωF​S)k{\rm{Vol}\,}(B^{4}(r),\omega_{0})<{\rm{Vol}\,}({\mathbb{C}}P^{2},\omega_{FS}), then there exists a symplectic embedding ⨆i=1kB4​(r)→(ℂ​P2∖Σ,ωF​S)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{4}(r)\to({\mathbb{C}}P^{2}\setminus\Sigma,\omega_{FS}).
Here is an extension of 3.2 to Kähler-type embeddings of balls into ℂ​P2{\mathbb{C}}P^{2}.
Theorem 3.15
A. Assume that 1≤k≤81\leq k\leq 8 and r1≥r2≥…≥rk>0r_{1}\geq r_{2}\geq\ldots\geq r_{k}>0.
Then for any proper (possibly empty) complex submanifold Σ⊂(ℂ​P2,Is​t)\Sigma\subset({\mathbb{C}}P^{2},I_{st}),
any symplectic embedding ⨆i=1kB4​(ri)→(ℂ​P2∖Σ,ωF​S)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{4}(r_{i})\to({\mathbb{C}}P^{2}\setminus\Sigma,\omega_{FS}) is, in fact, of [Is​t][I_{st}]-Kähler type:
it is holomorphic with respect to a complex structure on ℂ​P2{\mathbb{C}}P^{2} that is compatible with ωF​S\omega_{FS} and isotopic
to Is​tI_{st} by an isotopy fixing Σ\Sigma (as a set).
Accordingly, by part A of 3.2, such a Kähler-type embedding ⨆i=1kB4​(ri)→(ℂ​P2∖Σ,ωF​S)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{4}(r_{i})\to({\mathbb{C}}P^{2}\setminus\Sigma,\omega_{FS})
exists if and only if the radii r1,…,rkr_{1},\ldots,r_{k} satisfy the inequalities (v), (c1)-(c6) listed in part A of 3.2.
B. For any proper (possibly empty) complex submanifold Σ⊂(ℂ​P2,Is​t)\Sigma\subset({\mathbb{C}}P^{2},I_{st}) and any k=l2k=l^{2}, l∈ℤ>0l\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0},
any symplectic embedding ⨆i=1kB4​(r)→(ℂ​P2∖Σ,ωF​S)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{4}(r)\to({\mathbb{C}}P^{2}\setminus\Sigma,\omega_{FS}) is, in fact, of [Is​t][I_{st}]-Kähler type:
it is holomorphic with respect to a complex structure on ℂ​P2{\mathbb{C}}P^{2} that is compatible with ωF​S\omega_{FS}
and isotopic to Is​tI_{st} by an isotopy preserving Σ\Sigma (as a set).
Accordingly, by part B of 3.2, such Kähler-type embeddings ⨆i=1kB4​(r)→(ℂ​P2∖Σ,ωF​S)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{4}(r)\to({\mathbb{C}}P^{2}\setminus\Sigma,\omega_{FS}) are unobstructed: they exist if and only if k​Vol​(B4​(r),ω0)<Vol⁡(ℂ​P2,ωF​S)k{\rm{Vol}\,}(B^{4}(r),\omega_{0})<{\rm{Vol}\,}({\mathbb{C}}P^{2},\omega_{FS}).
C. Let Σ⊂(ℂ​P2,ωF​S)\Sigma\subset({\mathbb{C}}P^{2},\omega_{FS})
be either of the following:
(1) the empty set;
(2) a finite union of closed compact symplectic submanifolds (without boundary) of real dimension 2
whose pairwise intersections (if they exist) are transverse and ω\omega–orthogonal;
(3) a Lagrangian submanifold which is diffeomorphic to either 𝕊2{\mathbb{S}}^{2} or ℝ​P2{\mathbb{R}}P^{2}.
For any k∈ℤ>0k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0} and r1,…,rk>0r_{1},\ldots,r_{k}>0, any two Kähler-type embeddings ⨆i=1kB4​(ri)→(ℂ​P2∖Σ,ωF​S)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{4}(r_{i})\to({\mathbb{C}}P^{2}\setminus\Sigma,\omega_{FS}) (if they exist!) lie in the same orbit of the Symp0c​(ℂ​P2∖Σ,ωF​S)\mathrm{Symp}^{c}_{0}({\mathbb{C}}P^{2}\setminus\Sigma,\omega_{FS})-action, meaning that
the space of Kähler-type embeddings ⨆i=1kB4​(ri)→(ℂ​P2∖Σ,ωF​S)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{4}(r_{i})\to({\mathbb{C}}P^{2}\setminus\Sigma,\omega_{FS}) is connected.
Part B of 3.2 follows from
part A of 3.2
and from part (II) of 3.1.
Part C of 3.2 follows directly from part (I) of
3.1.
For the proof of part A of 3.2 see Section 6 (6).
Applying 3.2 with the hyperplane Σ=ℂ​P1⊂ℂ​P2\Sigma={\mathbb{C}}P^{1}\subset{\mathbb{C}}P^{2} and
recalling that (ℂ​P2∖ℂ​P1,ωF​S)({\mathbb{C}}P^{2}\setminus{\mathbb{C}}P^{1},\omega_{FS}) is symplectomorphic to (B4​(1),ω0)(B^{4}(1),\omega_{0}),
we get the following corollary.
Corollary 3.16
A. Assume that 1≤k≤81\leq k\leq 8 and r1≥r2≥…≥rk>0r_{1}\geq r_{2}\geq\ldots\geq r_{k}>0.
Then
any symplectic embedding ⨆i=1kB4​(ri)→(B4​(1),ω0)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{4}(r_{i})\to(B^{4}(1),\omega_{0})
is, in fact, of Kähler-type: it is holomorphic with respect to a complex structure on B4​(1)B^{4}(1) that is compatible with ω0\omega_{0} and isotopic
to I0I_{0}.
Accordingly, by part A of 3.2, such a Kähler-type embedding ⨆i=1kB4​(ri)→(B4​(1),ω0)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{4}(r_{i})\to(B^{4}(1),\omega_{0})
exists if and only if the radii r1,…,rkr_{1},\ldots,r_{k} satisfy the inequalities (v), (c1)-(c6) listed in part A of 3.2.
B. For any k=l2k=l^{2}, l∈ℤ>0l\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0},
any symplectic embedding ⨆i=1kB4​(r)→(B4​(1),ω0)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{4}(r)\to(B^{4}(1),\omega_{0}) is, in fact, of Kähler type:
it is holomorphic with respect to a complex structure on B4​(1)B^{4}(1) that is compatible with ω0\omega_{0}
and isotopic to I0I_{0}.
Accordingly, by part B of 3.2, such Kähler-type embeddings ⨆i=1kB4​(r)→(B4​(1),ω0)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{4}(r)\to(B^{4}(1),\omega_{0}) are unobstructed: they exist if and only if k​Vol​(B4​(r),ω0)<Vol⁡(B4​(1),ω0)k{\rm{Vol}\,}(B^{4}(r),\omega_{0})<{\rm{Vol}\,}(B^{4}(1),\omega_{0}).
C.
For any k∈ℤ>0k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0} and r1,…,rk>0r_{1},\ldots,r_{k}>0, any two Kähler-type embeddings ⨆i=1kB4​(ri)→(B4​(1),ω0)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{4}(r_{i})\to(B^{4}(1),\omega_{0}) (if they exist!) lie in the same orbit of the Symp0c​(B4​(1),ω0)\mathrm{Symp}^{c}_{0}(B^{4}(1),\omega_{0})-action, meaning that
the space of Kähler-type embeddings ⨆i=1kB4​(ri)→(B4​(1),ω0)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{4}(r_{i})\to(B^{4}(1),\omega_{0}) is connected.
Remark 3.17
1.
The description of the Kähler cone of the blow-up of ℂ​P2{\mathbb{C}}P^{2} at k≥10k\geq 10 (generic) points is a difficult open question related, in particular, to the Nagata conjecture
(which is equivalent to the claim that a certain cohomology class of the blow-up lies in the boundary of the Kähler cone,
see e.g. [Bir3]).
In particular, if there is a counterexample to the Nagata conjecture for k≥10k\geq 10, it
would yield that the Kähler-type embeddings of kk equal balls to ℂ​P2{\mathbb{C}}P^{2} are not unobstructed – see [McDP, Thm. 1.4.B]. Since by a theorem of Biran [Bir2], symplectic embeddings of a disjoint union of k≥10k\geq 10 equal balls into (ℂ​P2,ωF​S)({\mathbb{C}}P^{2},\omega_{FS}) are unobstructed, this would imply the existence of such
symplectic embeddings into (ℂ​P2,ωF​S)({\mathbb{C}}P^{2},\omega_{FS}) that are not of Kähler type.
2. The connectedness of the space of symplectic embeddings of ⨆i=1kB4​(ri)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{4}(r_{i}) into (ℂ​P2,ωF​S)({\mathbb{C}}P^{2},\omega_{FS}) and (B4​(1),ω0)(B^{4}(1),\omega_{0})
was proved in [McD3] (for previous partial results see [McD1, McD2, Lal, Bir1]).
3. By a theorem of Taubes [Tau1, Tau2, Tau3], any symplectic form on ℂ​P2{\mathbb{C}}P^{2} can be mapped into a positive multiple of ωF​S\omega_{FS} by a diffeomorphism of ℂ​P2{\mathbb{C}}P^{2}. Therefore, any symplectic form ω\omega on ℂ​P2{\mathbb{C}}P^{2} is of Kähler type and the results above stated for (ℂ​P2,ωF​S)({\mathbb{C}}P^{2},\omega_{FS})
hold also for (ℂ​P2,ω)({\mathbb{C}}P^{2},\omega).
3.3 The cases of ℂ​P2​♯​ℂ​P2¯{\mathbb{C}}P^{2}\sharp\overline{{\mathbb{C}}P^{2}} and ℂ​P1×ℂ​P1{\mathbb{C}}P^{1}\times{\mathbb{C}}P^{1}
Consider ℂ​P2{\mathbb{C}}P^{2} with the standard complex structure on it. Define M=ℂ​P2​♯​ℂ​P2¯M={\mathbb{C}}P^{2}\sharp\overline{{\mathbb{C}}P^{2}} – as a smooth manifold – as the complex blow-up of ℂ​P2{\mathbb{C}}P^{2} at one point. Let E⊂ME\subset M be the exceptional divisor and ℂ​P1⊂M{\mathbb{C}}P^{1}\subset M a projective line.
Given 0<λ<π0<\lambda<\pi, let ωλ\omega_{\lambda} be a Kähler form on MM such that ⟨[ωλ],[ℂ​P1]⟩=π\langle[\omega_{\lambda}],[{\mathbb{C}}P^{1}]\rangle=\pi,
⟨[ωλ],[E]⟩=λ\langle[\omega_{\lambda}],[E]\rangle=\lambda.
The manifold M=ℂ​P2​♯​ℂ​P2¯M={\mathbb{C}}P^{2}\sharp\overline{{\mathbb{C}}P^{2}} admits Kähler-type complex structures J2​l+1J_{2l+1}, l∈ℤ≥0l\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}, such that (M,J2​l+1)(M,J_{2l+1}) is biholomorphic to the odd Hirzebruch surface 𝔽2​l+1{\mathbb{F}}_{2l+1}. In particular, we assume J1=I~s​tJ_{1}={\widetilde{I}}_{st}, as we identify the Hirzebruch surface 𝔽1{\mathbb{F}}_{1} with (ℂ​P~Is​t,x2,I~s​t)(\widetilde{{\mathbb{C}}P}^{2}_{I_{st},x},{\widetilde{I}}_{st}). Each complex structure on MM can be mapped by a diffeomorphism into exactly one complex structure J2​l+1J_{2l+1} – this follows from [FriQ]. In fact, this diffeomorphism can be chosen to lie in Diff0​(M)\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M) – see [Mee].

Now let M=ℂ​P1×ℂ​P1M={\mathbb{C}}P^{1}\times{\mathbb{C}}P^{1} – as a smooth manifold. Let

Remark 3.21
1. For Σ=(pt×ℂ​P1)∪(ℂ​P1×pt)\Sigma=(\textrm{pt}\times{\mathbb{C}}P^{1})\cup({\mathbb{C}}P^{1}\times\textrm{pt}) the manifold (M∖Σ,ωμ)(M\setminus\Sigma,\omega_{\mu})
is symplectomorphic to (B2​(1)×B2​(μ),ω0⊕ω0)(B^{2}(1)\times B^{2}(\sqrt{\mu}),\omega_{0}\oplus\omega_{0}). Therefore 3.3 yields a straightforward corollary concerning Kähler-type embeddings of balls into (B2​(1)×B2​(μ),ω0⊕ω0)(B^{2}(1)\times B^{2}(\sqrt{\mu}),\omega_{0}\oplus\omega_{0}).
For symplectic embeddings of balls a similar corollary, as well as the result of part B of 3.3,
were proved in [McDP].
2. We do not know under which conditions on r1,…,rkr_{1},\ldots,r_{k} the complex structure J0J_{0} on MM can be replaced in part A of 3.3 by
J2​lJ_{2l}, l>0l>0.
In order to obtain these conditions one needs to get an explicit description of the Kähler cones
of the blow-ups of the Hirzebruch surfaces 𝔽2​l{\mathbb{F}}_{2l}, l>0l>0, at kk points – see Section 8.
3. For any R1,…,RkR_{1},\ldots,R_{k} the connectedness of the space of symplectic embeddings ⨆i=1kB4​(Ri)→(M,ωλ)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{4}(R_{i})\to(M,\omega_{\lambda}) was proved in [McD3].
3.4 The case of tori and K3 surfaces
Let MM, dimℝM=2​n\dim_{\mathbb{R}}M=2n, be either 𝕋2​n{\mathbb{T}}^{2n} or a smooth manifold (of real dimension 44) underlying a complex K3 surface.
As before, let ω\omega be a Kähler-type symplectic form on MM.
In the case M=𝕋2​nM={\mathbb{T}}^{2n} one can assume, without loss of generality, that ω\omega is linear (see 2.1).
Recall that ω\omega is called rational if the real cohomology class [ω]∈H2​(M,ℝ)[\omega]\in H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{R}}) is a real multiple of a rational one,
and irrational otherwise.
Irrational Kähler-type symplectic forms on tori and K3 surfaces are compatible with so-called Campana-simple complex structures. Namely, given a Kähler-type complex structure II on a closed manifold MM, the union of all positive-dimensional proper complex
subvarieties of (M,I)(M,I) is
either a countable union of proper analytic subvarieties of MM (and hence has a dense connected complement) or the whole MM (see e.g. [EV1, Remark 4.2]).
In the former case, the complex structure II is called Campana-simple [Cam], [CaDV] (for more details see Section 9).
In fact, a Kähler-type complex structure on a torus is Campana-simple if and only if it has no proper positive-dimensional complex subvarieties (see 10.1). A symplectic form ω\omega on the torus or a K3 surface is compatible with such a complex structure if and only if ω\omega is irrational (see 10.1 and 10.2).
See Section 3.5 for other examples of manifolds admitting Campana-simple complex structures.
Theorem 3.22
Let MM, dimℝM=2​n\dim_{\mathbb{R}}M=2n, be either 𝕋2​n{\mathbb{T}}^{2n} or a smooth manifold (of real dimension 44) underlying a complex K3 surface
and let ω\omega be a Kähler-type symplectic form on MM.
Assume that ω\omega is irrational.
Let k∈ℤ>0k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}, r1,…,rk>0r_{1},\ldots,r_{k}>0.
Then the following claims hold:
A. Kähler-type embeddings of ⨆i=1kB2​n​(ri)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{2n}(r_{i}) into (M,ω)(M,\omega) are unobstructed.
More precisely, assume that Vol⁡(⨆i=1kB2​n​(ri),ω0)<Vol⁡(M,ω){\rm{Vol}\,}\left(\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{2n}(r_{i}),\omega_{0}\right)<{\rm{Vol}\,}(M,\omega) and ω\omega is
compatible with a Campana-simple complex structure II.
Then
there exists an [I][I]-Kähler-type embedding ⨆i=1kB2​n​(ri)→(M,ω)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{2n}(r_{i})\to(M,\omega).
B.
The group SympH​(M,ω)\mathrm{Symp}_{H}(M,\omega) acts transitively on the set of connected components of
𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M) compatible with ω\omega.
C. Any two Kähler-type embeddings ⨆i=1kB2​n​(ri)→(M,ω)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{2n}(r_{i})\to(M,\omega) (if they exist!) lie in the same orbit of the
SympH​(M,ω)\mathrm{Symp}_{H}(M,\omega)-action. They lie in the same orbit of the Symp⁡(M,ω)∩Diff0​(M)\mathrm{Symp}(M,\omega)\cap\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M)-action
if and only if they favor a common connected component of 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M). In the latter case there exists [I]∈𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑⁡(M,ω)[I]\in{\tt Teich}(M,\omega)
such that both embeddings are of [I][I]-Kähler-type.
Remark 3.23
Assume M=𝕋2M={\mathbb{T}}^{2} is a torus of real dimension 22. In this case all symplectic forms and complex structures on 𝕋2{\mathbb{T}}^{2} are of Kähler-type and
any symplectic (i.e.,
area-preserving) embedding into 𝕋2{\mathbb{T}}^{2} is of Kähler-type. In particular, for any symplectic form ω\omega on 𝕋2{\mathbb{T}}^{2} (note that any such form is rational!) the result of part A of 3.4 is true
and the space of Kähler-type embeddings of any disjoint union of domains into (𝕋2,ω)({\mathbb{T}}^{2},\omega) is path-connected
(since the space of symplectic forms on 𝕋2{\mathbb{T}}^{2} is path-connected [Mos]).
For the proof
of 3.4
see Section 10.3 (10.3).
For a generalization of 3.4 to arbitrary
closed symplectic manifolds admitting Campana-simple complex structures compatible with the symplectic form see 9. In fact, we will use 9 in the proof of 3.4.
For tori the existence result in 3.4 admits the following equivalent formulation.
Corollary 3.24
Assume Γ⊂ℂn\Gamma\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{n} is a lattice of rank 2​n2n. Let M:=ℂn/ΓM:={\mathbb{C}}^{n}/\Gamma be the corresponding torus. Let ω,I\omega,I be the symplectic and the complex structures on MM induced by the standard flat symplectic and complex structures on ℂn=ℝ2​n{\mathbb{C}}^{n}={\mathbb{R}}^{2n}.
Assume that (M,I)(M,I) admits no proper positive-dimensional
complex subvarieties. Let k∈ℤ>0k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0},
r1,…,rk>0r_{1},\ldots,r_{k}>0 such that
Vol⁡(⨆i=1kB2​n​(ri),ω0)<Vol⁡(M,ω){\rm{Vol}\,}\left(\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{2n}(r_{i}),\omega_{0}\right)<{\rm{Vol}\,}(M,\omega).
Then there exists a Kähler form ω′\omega^{\prime} on (M,I)(M,I) isotopic to ω\omega and a Kähler-type embedding
⨆i=1kB2​n​(ri)→(M,ω′)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{2n}(r_{i})\to(M,\omega^{\prime}) which is holomorphic with respect to II – i.e., an embedding which is symplectic with respect
to ω′\omega^{\prime} and holomorphic with respect to II.
Proof of 3.4:
It suffices to prove a similar claim for the case where Γ=ℤ2​n\Gamma={\mathbb{Z}}^{2n} and ω\omega and II are compatible symplectic and complex structures on 𝕋2​n=ℝ2​n/ℤ2​n{\mathbb{T}}^{2n}={\mathbb{R}}^{2n}/{\mathbb{Z}}^{2n}, with II being Campana-simple (the proof can be reduced to this case by using
a diffeomorphism M=ℂn/Γ→𝕋2​n=ℝ2​n/ℤ2​nM={\mathbb{C}}^{n}/\Gamma\to{\mathbb{T}}^{2n}={\mathbb{R}}^{2n}/{\mathbb{Z}}^{2n}
induced by an ℝ{\mathbb{R}}-linear isomorphism of ℝ2​n=ℂ2​n{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}={\mathbb{C}}^{2n} sending Γ\Gamma to ℤ2​n{\mathbb{Z}}^{2n}). In order to prove the corollary in this case, apply part A of 3.4 and
keep the complex structure fixed while changing the symplectic form by an isotopy
(cf. 2.3).
Remark 3.25
1. For an arbitrary Kähler-type symplectic form ω\omega on M=𝕋2​nM={\mathbb{T}}^{2n} or on a manifold MM underlying a K3 surface,
the unobstructedness of symplectic – but not necessarily Kähler-type or tame – embeddings of an arbitrary collection of disjoint balls
was proved in [EV1, Thm. 3.1]. In the case M=𝕋4M={\mathbb{T}}^{4} a weaker version of this result had been previously proved by Latschev-McDuff-Schlenk [LatMS].
2. It is a well-known open problem (see e.g. [McDS, Ch.15, Prob. 15]) whether SympH​(𝕋2​n,ω)=Symp0​(𝕋2​n,ω)\mathrm{Symp}_{H}({\mathbb{T}}^{2n},\omega)=\mathrm{Symp}_{0}({\mathbb{T}}^{2n},\omega)
for a Kähler-type symplectic form ω\omega on 𝕋2​n{\mathbb{T}}^{2n}, n>1n>1.
If the answer to this question is positive, then
Part B of
3.4
would imply that the space of the Kähler-type embeddings ⨆i=1kB2​n​(ri)→(𝕋2​n,ω)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{2n}(r_{i})\to({\mathbb{T}}^{2n},\omega)
is path-connected for any k∈ℤ>0k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0} and r1,…,rk>0r_{1},\ldots,r_{k}>0.
Conversely, a counter-example to the latter claim would show that
SympH​(𝕋2​n,ω)≠Symp0​(𝕋2​n,ω)\mathrm{Symp}_{H}({\mathbb{T}}^{2n},\omega)\neq\mathrm{Symp}_{0}({\mathbb{T}}^{2n},\omega).
In the case when MM is a manifold underlying a K3 surface it is known by the result of Sheridan-Smith [ShS]
that SympH​(M,ω)≠Symp0​(M,ω)\mathrm{Symp}_{H}(M,\omega)\neq\mathrm{Symp}_{0}(M,\omega) for certain
Kähler-type symplectic structures ω\omega on MM,
including some irrational ones.
It has been known since the work [Sei] of Seidel that for some (not necessarily irrational) Kähler-type symplectic forms ω\omega on MM one has Symp⁡(M,ω)∩Diff0​(M)≠Symp0​(M,ω)\mathrm{Symp}(M,\omega)\cap\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M)\neq\mathrm{Symp}_{0}(M,\omega); by a result of Smirnov [Sm], this definitely holds for some irrational Kähler-type symplectic forms ω\omega on MM.
3. 3.4 for one ellipsoid – and, in particular, for one ball – was previously proved by Luef and Wang [LuWa] using a method similar to the one in this paper.
Let us note that the paper [LuWa] by Luef and Wang provides a very interesting connection between Kähler-type embeddings of ellipsoids into tori and Gabor frames – an important notion in signal processing.
We expect that the technique used in [LuWa], as well as the
techniques developed in [EV2] in relation to symplectic embeddings of ellipsoids into tori and K3 surfaces,
can be used to generalize 3.4
to Kähler-type embeddings of ellipsoids.
Remark 3.26
The assumption that the symplectic form ω\omega on MM is irrational is essential in 3.4: otherwise there may be obstructions
for the existence of Kähler-type embeddings of (disjoint unions of) balls into (M,ω)(M,\omega) that
are independent of the symplectic volume – for instance, the obstructions coming from Seshadri constants.
For simplicity, we will explain this below in the case of one ball and M=𝕋2​nM={\mathbb{T}}^{2n},
expanding on [LatMS, Sec. 2.2].
First, recall the definition of Seshadri constants. Let (X,I)(X,I) be a complex projective manifold and let ω\omega be a Kähler form on (X,I)(X,I)
such that the cohomology class [ω][\omega] is integral and equals the first Chern class of an ample line bundle on (X,I)(X,I).
Assume that x∈Xx\in X, π:X~→X\pi:{\widetilde{X}}\to X is the complex blow-up of XX at xx, and E:=π−1​(x)E:=\pi^{-1}(x) is the exceptional divisor.
Define the Seshadri constant
ϵ(X,I,[ω],x):=sup{s>0|⟨π∗[ω],[C~]⟩−sE⋅[C~]≥0\epsilon(X,I,[\omega],x):=\sup\left\{s>0\ \left|\right.\ \left\langle\pi^{*}[\omega],[{\widetilde{C}}]\right\rangle-sE\cdot[{\widetilde{C}}]\geq 0\right.
for all (possibly singular) complex curvesC~⊂X~}.\left.\textrm{for all (possibly singular) complex curves}\ {\widetilde{C}}\subset{\widetilde{X}}\ \right\}.
Observe that E⋅[C~]E\cdot[{\widetilde{C}}] is the multiplicity of the singularity of CC at xx.
Then
ϵ⁡(X,I,[ω],x)=\displaystyle\epsilon(X,I,[\omega],x)=
sup{πr2|There exists a Kähler formω′,[ω′]=[ω],\displaystyle\sup\left\{\ \pi r^{2}\ |\ \textrm{There exists a K\"{a}hler form}\ \omega^{\prime},\ [\omega^{\prime}]=[\omega],\right.
and a Kähler embedding​f:B2​n​(r)→(X,ω′,I),\displaystyle\textrm{and a K\"{a}hler embedding}\ f:B^{2n}(r)\to(X,\omega^{\prime},I),
(3.1)
f∗ω′=ω0,f∗I=J0,f(0)=x}.\displaystyle\left.f^{*}\omega^{\prime}=\omega_{0},\ f^{*}I=J_{0},\ f(0)=x\ \right\}.
For a proof see [Ec, Thm. 0.6], [Fl, Thm. 1.4], [WN2, Thm. 1.3], cf. [LuWa, Thm. A]. (It had been previously observed in [Laz] that the inequality ϵ⁡(X,I,[ω],x)≥\epsilon(X,I,[\omega],x)\geq [the right-hand side in (3.1)] follows from the results of [McDP]).
Assume that ω\omega is a rational symplectic form on 𝕋2​n{\mathbb{T}}^{2n}. For the study of the symplectic and Kähler-type embeddings in (𝕋2​n,ω)({\mathbb{T}}^{2n},\omega) we may assume then, without loss of generality, that [ω]∈H2​(𝕋2​n,ℤ)[\omega]\in H^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{2n};{\mathbb{Z}}). Let II be a complex structure on 𝕋2​n{\mathbb{T}}^{2n}
compatible with ω\omega. Consider an ample holomorphic bundle LL on 𝕋2​n{\mathbb{T}}^{2n} such that c1​(L)=[ω]c_{1}(L)=[\omega].
The complex manifold (𝕋2​n,I)({\mathbb{T}}^{2n},I) can be viewed as an abelian variety and LL as its polarization.
Since the
identity component of the group of biholomorphisms of a complex torus acts transitively on the torus,
ϵ⁡(𝕋2​n,I,[ω],x)\epsilon({\mathbb{T}}^{2n},I,[\omega],x) depends only on II and [ω][\omega] and not on x∈𝕋2​nx\in{\mathbb{T}}^{2n}, so we will denote ϵ⁡(𝕋2​n,I,[ω]):=ϵ⁡(𝕋2​n,I,[ω],x)\epsilon({\mathbb{T}}^{2n},I,[\omega]):=\epsilon({\mathbb{T}}^{2n},I,[\omega],x).
Since Vol⁡(B2​n​(r),ω0)=πn​r2​n{\rm{Vol}\,}(B^{2n}(r),\omega_{0})=\pi^{n}r^{2n}, (3.1) implies that
sup{Vol(B2​n(r),ω0)|There exists a Kähler-type embedding\sup\left\{\ {\rm{Vol}\,}(B^{2n}(r),\omega_{0})\ \left|\right.\ \textrm{There exists a K\"{a}hler-type embedding}\right.
f:(B2​n(r),ω0)→(𝕋2​n,ω)}=supI∈𝐶𝑚𝑝𝑡⁡(M,ω)ϵn(𝕋2​n,I,[ω]),\left.f:(B^{2n}(r),\omega_{0})\to({\mathbb{T}}^{2n},\omega)\ \right\}=\sup_{I\in{\it Cmpt}(M,\omega)}\epsilon^{n}({\mathbb{T}}^{2n},I,[\omega]),
where the supremum in the right-hand side is taken over all II compatible with ω\omega.
There exist examples where
supI∈𝐶𝑚𝑝𝑡⁡(M,ω)ϵn​(𝕋2​n,I,[ω])<Vol⁡(𝕋2​n,ω).\sup_{I\in{\it Cmpt}(M,\omega)}\epsilon^{n}({\mathbb{T}}^{2n},I,[\omega])<{\rm{Vol}\,}({\mathbb{T}}^{2n},\omega).
For instance, consider the torus 𝕋4=ℝ4/ℤ4{\mathbb{T}}^{4}={\mathbb{R}}^{4}/{\mathbb{Z}}^{4} equipped with the symplectic form ω=d​p1∧d​q1+d​p2∧d​q2\omega=dp_{1}\wedge dq_{1}+dp_{2}\wedge dq_{2}, where p1,p2,q1,q2p_{1},p_{2},q_{1},q_{2} are the coordinates on ℝ4{\mathbb{R}}^{4}. For any complex structure II on 𝕋4{\mathbb{T}}^{4} compatible with ω\omega one can biholomorphically identify (𝕋4,I)({\mathbb{T}}^{4},I) with a principally polarized abelian variety. In this case, by a result of Steffens [Ste, Prop. 2], we have ϵ⁡(𝕋4,I,[ω])≤4/3\epsilon({\mathbb{T}}^{4},I,[\omega])\leq 4/3 for all II compatible with ω\omega. At the same time Vol⁡(𝕋4,ω)=∫𝕋4ω2=2{\rm{Vol}\,}({\mathbb{T}}^{4},\omega)=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{4}}\omega^{2}=2. Thus,
supI∈𝐶𝑚𝑝𝑡⁡(M,ω)ϵ2​(𝕋4,I,[ω])≤(4/3)2<2=Vol⁡(𝕋4,ω).\sup_{I\in{\it Cmpt}(M,\omega)}\epsilon^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{4},I,[\omega])\leq(4/3)^{2}<2={\rm{Vol}\,}({\mathbb{T}}^{4},\omega).
This yields an obstruction, independent of the symplectic volume, for the existence of Kähler-type embeddings of a ball into (𝕋4,ω)({\mathbb{T}}^{4},\omega):
a ball whose volume is between (4/3)2(4/3)^{2} and 2=Vol⁡(𝕋4,ω)2={\rm{Vol}\,}({\mathbb{T}}^{4},\omega) does not admit Kähler-type embeddings into (𝕋4,ω)({\mathbb{T}}^{4},\omega).
At the same time, by
[LatMS, Thm. 1.1],
such a ball does admit a
symplectic embedding into (𝕋4,ω)({\mathbb{T}}^{4},\omega). Thus, there exists
a symplectic embedding of a ball which is not of Kähler type.
We do not know whether such an embedding is tame.
Let us now consider tame embeddings of balls into tori and K3 surfaces.
Theorem 3.27
Let MM, dimℝM=2​n\dim_{\mathbb{R}}M=2n, be either 𝕋2​n{\mathbb{T}}^{2n} or a smooth manifold (of real dimension 44) underlying a complex K3 surface.
Let ω1\omega_{1}, ω2\omega_{2} be
Kähler-type forms on MM that are irrational and satisfy
∫Mω1n=∫Mω2n>0\int_{M}\omega_{1}^{n}=\int_{M}\omega_{2}^{n}>0.
