is −Pos⁡(M,I)-{\rm{Pos}\,}(M,I).
Let G​r+⁣+​(H2​(M,ℝ))Gr_{++}(H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{R}})) be the Grassmanian of positive oriented 22-dimensional planes in
H2​(M,ℝ)H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{R}}).
There is a canonical diffeomorphism identifying G​r+⁣+​(H2​(M,ℝ))Gr_{++}(H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{R}}))
with an open subset {l∈ℙH2(M;ℂ)|q(l,l)=0,q(l,l¯)>0}\{l\in{\mathbb{P}}H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{C}})\ |\ q(l,l)=0,\ q(l,{\bar{l}})>0\}
of the quadric {l∈ℙ​H2​(M,ℂ)|q⁡(l,l)=0}\{l\in{\mathbb{P}}H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{C}})\ |\ q(l,l)=0\}
(see e.g. [Huyb, Ch.6, Prop. 1.5] and [Ver6, Claim 2.9]).
Thus G​r+⁣+​(H2​(M,ℝ))Gr_{++}(H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{R}})) has the structure of a connected complex manifold.
Define the period map 𝖯𝖾𝗋:𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑⁡(M)→G​r+⁣+​(H2​(M,ℝ))\operatorname{\sf Per}:{\tt Teich}(M)\to Gr_{++}(H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{R}})) by
𝖯𝖾𝗋⁡([I]):=WI,\operatorname{\sf Per}([I]):=W_{I},
where [I]∈𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑⁡(M)[I]\in{\tt Teich}(M) is the element of 𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑⁡(M){\tt Teich}(M) represented by II.
The global Torelli theorem for K3 surfaces (see [Huyb] and the references therein to the original papers)
implies the following claim.
Theorem 10.12
A. There exists a subset 𝒮⊂𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑⁡(M){\mathcal{S}}\subset{\tt Teich}(M) so that for each connected component 𝒞0{\mathcal{C}}_{0} of 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M)
the intersection 𝒮∩𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M){\mathcal{S}}\cap{\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M) is dense and connected in 𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M){\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M)
and the map
𝖯𝖾𝗋:𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M)→G​r+⁣+​(H2​(M,ℝ))\operatorname{\sf Per}:{\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M)\to Gr_{++}(H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{R}}))
is a surjective local biholomorphism which is injective on 𝒮∩𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M){\mathcal{S}}\cap{\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M).
B. Assume that I0,I1∈𝐶𝐾⁡(M)I_{0},I_{1}\in\mathit{CK}(M), [I0],[I1]∈𝒮[I_{0}],[I_{1}]\in{\mathcal{S}} and 𝖯𝖾𝗋⁡([I0])=𝖯𝖾𝗋⁡([I1])\operatorname{\sf Per}([I_{0}])=\operatorname{\sf Per}([I_{1}]).
Then there exists a unique diffeomorphism ϕ∈DiffH​(M)\phi\in\mathrm{Diff}_{H}(M) such that ϕ∗​I1=I0\phi^{*}I_{1}=I_{0}.
By a (−2)(-2)-class we will mean an integral cohomology class b∈H2​(M,ℤ)b\in H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{Z}}) such that q⁡(b,b)=−2q(b,b)=-2.
For each I∈𝐶𝐾⁡(M)I\in\mathit{CK}(M) denote by S⁡(M,I)S(M,I) the set of all (−2)(-2)-classes b∈H2​(M,ℤ)b\in H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{Z}}) that are of type (1,1)(1,1) with respect to II.
The Kähler cone of (M,I)(M,I) admits the following description.
Theorem 10.13 (see e.g. [Huyb, Sec. 8, Thm. 5.2], cf. [AmV2, Thm. 6.2])
The Kähler cone of (M,I)(M,I) is a connected component of the set
Pos(M,I)∖∪b∈S⁡(M,I)(b⊥∩Pos(M,I)).{\rm{Pos}\,}(M,I)\setminus\cup_{b\in S(M,I)}\left(b^{\bot}\cap{\rm{Pos}\,}(M,I)\right).
In particular, if b⊥∩Pos⁡(M,I)=∅b^{\bot}\cap{\rm{Pos}\,}(M,I)=\emptyset for all b∈S⁡(M,I)b\in S(M,I), then the Kähler cone of (M,I)(M,I) is just Pos⁡(M,I){\rm{Pos}\,}(M,I).
The next result follows from the Calabi-Yau theorem (formerly the Calabi conjecture) [Yau2] combined with Bochner’s technique – see [Bea1] (cf. e.g. [GHJ, Thm. 5.11]) for the proof of the existence and uniqueness; see [FuS, Thm. 6.3] for the proof of the smooth dependence.
Theorem 10.14
Let I∈𝐶𝐾⁡(M)I\in\mathit{CK}(M) and let a∈H2​(M,ℝ)a\in H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{R}}) be a Kähler class with respect to II.
Then there exists a unique η∈𝑆𝐾⁡(M)\eta\in\mathit{SK}(M) compatible with II such that [η]=a[\eta]=a and the Riemannian metric η(⋅,I⋅)\eta(\cdot,I\cdot)
is Ricci-flat. The form η\eta depends smoothly on the pair (I,a)(I,a) (as II varies in a complex-analytic deformation family).
Remark 10.15
For any Kähler structure (η,I)(\eta,I) on MM such that the Riemannian metric η(⋅,I⋅)\eta(\cdot,I\cdot)
is Ricci-flat,
the form η\eta and the complex structure II can be included together in a hyperkähler structure (ω1:=η,ω2,ω3,I1:=I,I2,I3)(\omega_{1}:=\eta,\omega_{2},\omega_{3},I_{1}:=I,I_{2},I_{3}).
Proposition 10.16
Let I∈𝐶𝐾⁡(M)I\in\mathit{CK}(M) be compatible with ω\omega.
Then there exists J∈𝐶𝐾⁡(M)J\in\mathit{CK}(M) isotopic to II,
so that the Riemannian metric ω(⋅,J⋅)\omega(\cdot,J\cdot) is Ricci-flat.
Proof of 10.2:
By 10.2, the complex structure II is a part of a (unique) Kähler structure (η,I)(\eta,I) on MM for which the corresponding Riemannian metric η(⋅,I⋅)\eta(\cdot,I\cdot) is Ricci-flat, and moreover [η]=[ω][\eta]=[\omega]. The symplectic forms ω\omega and η\eta are cohomologous and compatible with the same II and therefore, by Moser’s theorem [Mos], there exists ϕ∈Diff0​(M)\phi\in\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M) such that ϕ∗​η=ω\phi^{*}\eta=\omega. Then J:=ϕ∗​IJ:=\phi^{*}I is compatible with ω\omega and the Riemannian metric ω(⋅,J⋅)\omega(\cdot,J\cdot) is Ricci-flat.
Let ω\omega be a Kähler-type symplectic form on MM.
Define Dω⊂G​r+⁣+​(H2​(M,ℝ)){\it D}_{\omega}\subset Gr_{++}\left(H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{R}})\right) as the set of positive oriented 22-dimensional planes W⊂H2​(M,ℝ)W\subset H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{R}}) such that [ω]∈W⊥[\omega]\in W^{\bot}
and (ℝ⁡[ω]⊕W)⊥({\mathbb{R}}[\omega]\oplus W)^{\bot} does not contain any (−2)(-2)-classes.
Proposition 10.17
The set Dω{\it D}_{\omega} is a dense connected subset of the submanifold G​r+⁣+​([ω]⊥)Gr_{++}\left([\omega]^{\bot}\right) of G​r+⁣+​(H2​(M,ℝ))Gr_{++}\left(H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{R}})\right).
Proof of 10.2:
We have q⁡([ω],[ω])>0q([\omega],[\omega])>0. The restriction of qq to [ω]⊥[\omega]^{\bot} is a quadratic form of signature (2,19)(2,19).
The space G​r+⁣+​([ω]⊥)Gr_{++}([\omega]^{\bot}) of positive oriented 22-dimensional planes in [ω]⊥[\omega]^{\bot} is a smooth connected manifold diffeomorphic to
S​O​(2,19)/S​O​(2)×S​O​(19)SO(2,19)/SO(2)\times SO(19).
If a∈H2​(M,ℤ)a\in H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{Z}}) is a (−2)(-2)-class, then aa and [ω][\omega] are linearly independent and the restriction of qq to a⊥∩[ω]⊥a^{\bot}\cap[\omega]^{\bot} has signature (2,18)(2,18). The space G​r+⁣+​(a⊥∩[ω]⊥)Gr_{++}\left(a^{\bot}\cap[\omega]^{\bot}\right) of positive oriented 22-dimensional planes in
a⊥∩[ω]⊥a^{\bot}\cap[\omega]^{\bot}
is a closed submanifold of G​r+⁣+​([ω]⊥)Gr_{++}\left([\omega]^{\bot}\right) diffeomorphic to
S​O​(2,18)/S​O​(2)×S​O​(18)SO(2,18)/SO(2)\times SO(18) and thus has real codimension 22 in G​r+⁣+​([ω]⊥)Gr_{++}\left([\omega]^{\bot}\right).
The set Dω{\it D}_{\omega} is the complement in G​r+⁣+​([ω]⊥)Gr_{++}\left([\omega]^{\bot}\right) of the (at most) countable union of the closed codimension-22 submanifolds G​r+⁣+​(a⊥∩[ω]⊥)Gr_{++}\left(a^{\bot}\cap[\omega]^{\bot}\right) for all (−2)(-2)-classes a∈H2​(M,ℤ)a\in H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{Z}}) such that q⁡(a,[ω])=0q(a,[\omega])=0. Therefore Dω{\it D}_{\omega} is a dense connected subset of the submanifold G​r+⁣+​([ω]⊥)Gr_{++}\left([\omega]^{\bot}\right) of G​r+⁣+​(H2​(M,ℝ))Gr_{++}\left(H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{R}})\right).
(The density follows from Baire’s theorem; for a proof of the connectivity see e.g. [Ver6, Lem. 4.10].)
Proposition 10.18
Assume that 𝒞0{\mathcal{C}}_{0} is a connected component of 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M) compatible with the symplectic form ω\omega (i.e., 𝒞0{\mathcal{C}}_{0} contains a complex structure compatible with ω\omega).
Then 𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M,ω)=𝖯𝖾𝗋−1⁡(Dω)∩𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M){\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M,\omega)=\operatorname{\sf Per}^{-1}({\it D}_{\omega})\cap{\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M) and it is
a dense connected subset
of the closed submanifold 𝖯𝖾𝗋−1⁡(G​r+⁣+​([ω]⊥))∩𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M)\operatorname{\sf Per}^{-1}\left(Gr_{++}\left([\omega]^{\bot}\right)\right)\cap{\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M) of 𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M){\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M).
Proof of 10.2:
Let us show that 𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M,ω)⊂𝖯𝖾𝗋−1⁡(Dω)∩𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M){\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M,\omega)\subset\operatorname{\sf Per}^{-1}({\it D}_{\omega})\cap{\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M).
Consider a complex structure I∈𝒞0I\in{\mathcal{C}}_{0} compatible with ω\omega. Then [ω]∈HI1,1​(M,ℝ)[\omega]\in H_{I}^{1,1}(M;{\mathbb{R}}).
Therefore WIW_{I} is orthogonal to [ω][\omega].
Moreover, ℝ⁡[ω]⊕WI{\mathbb{R}}[\omega]\oplus W_{I} cannot contain any (−2)(-2)-class in its orthogonal complement: indeed, if a∈H2​(M,ℤ)a\in H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{Z}}) is
orthogonal to (HI2,0​(M,ℂ)⊕HI0,2​(M,ℂ))∩H∗​(M,ℝ)(H_{I}^{2,0}(M;{\mathbb{C}})\oplus H_{I}^{0,2}(M;{\mathbb{C}}))\cap H^{*}(M;{\mathbb{R}}), then a∈HI1,1​(M,ℝ)a\in H^{1,1}_{I}(M;{\mathbb{R}}). However, by 10.2, a (−2)(-2)-class in HI1,1​(M,ℝ)H_{I}^{1,1}(M;{\mathbb{R}}) cannot be orthogonal to a Kähler class with respect to II. Thus, we have proved that 𝖯𝖾𝗋⁡([I])=WI\operatorname{\sf Per}([I])=W_{I} belongs to Dω{\it D}_{\omega}. This yields that 𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M,ω)⊂𝖯𝖾𝗋−1⁡(Dω)∩𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M){\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M,\omega)\subset\operatorname{\sf Per}^{-1}({\it D}_{\omega})\cap{\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M).
Now let us show that 𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M,ω)⊃𝖯𝖾𝗋−1⁡(Dω)∩𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M){\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M,\omega)\supset\operatorname{\sf Per}^{-1}({\it D}_{\omega})\cap{\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M).
By the hypothesis of the proposition, there exists a complex structure I∈𝒞0I\in{\mathcal{C}}_{0} compatible with ω\omega.
In view of 10.2, we may assume, without loss of generality, that the metric ω(⋅,I⋅)\omega(\cdot,I\cdot) is Ricci-flat.
Let WW be an arbitrary plane belonging to Dω{\it D}_{\omega}. By 10.2, it can be connected with WIW_{I}
by a smooth path of planes in Dω{\it D}_{\omega}. Since 𝖯𝖾𝗋\operatorname{\sf Per} is a local biholomorphism (by part A of 10.2) and
by the above-mentioned path-lifting property of the 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)→𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M)\to{\tt Teich}(M), this path can be lifted to a smooth path in 𝒞0{\mathcal{C}}_{0} – i.e.,
there exists a smooth path
of complex structures {It}\{I_{t}\}, 0≤t≤10\leq t\leq 1, in 𝒞0{\mathcal{C}}_{0} such that I0=II_{0}=I and WI1=WW_{I_{1}}=W and WIt∈DωW_{I_{t}}\in{\it D}_{\omega} for each t∈[0,1]t\in[0,1].
Consequently, for each t∈[0,1]t\in[0,1] the plane WItW_{I_{t}} is orthogonal to [ω][\omega] and (ℝ⁡[ω]⊕W)⊥({\mathbb{R}}[\omega]\oplus W)^{\bot} does not contain any (−2)(-2)-classes. Therefore, by 10.2, for each t∈[0,1]t\in[0,1] either [ω][\omega] or −[ω]-[\omega] lies in the Kähler cone of (M,It)(M,I_{t}).
We claim that it is always [ω][\omega], and not −[ω]-[\omega].
Indeed, consider the set AA of t∈[0,1]t\in[0,1] for which it is [ω][\omega].
The set AA is non-empty since 0∈A0\in A: the cohomology class [ω][\omega] is Kähler with respect to I=I0I=I_{0}.
The set AA is also open:
if for some t0∈[0,1]t_{0}\in[0,1] the class [ω][\omega] is Kähler with respect
to It0I_{t_{0}}, then for any t∈[0,1]t\in[0,1] sufficiently close to t0t_{0}, the class [ω]It1,1=[ω][\omega]_{I_{t}}^{1,1}=[\omega] is Kähler with
respect to ItI_{t} (by the corollary of the Kodaira-Spencer stability theorem [EV1, Thm. 5.6], see Section 13.2).
For the same reason the complement of AA in [0,1][0,1] (i.e., the set of t∈[0,1]t\in[0,1] for which −[ω]-[\omega] is Kähler with respect to ItI_{t}) is also open. Thus, AA is open, closed and non-empty, meaning that A=[0,1]A=[0,1], which proves the claim.
Thus, for each t∈[0,1]t\in[0,1] the class [ω][\omega] is Kähler with respect to a Kähler-type complex structure ItI_{t} depending smoothly on tt
and for t=0t=0 the metric ω(⋅,I0⋅)\omega(\cdot,I_{0}\cdot) is Ricci-flat. By 10.2, there exists a smooth family of cohomologous symplectic forms ωt\omega_{t}, 0≤t≤10\leq t\leq 1, such that ω0=ω\omega_{0}=\omega and ωt\omega_{t} is compatible with ItI_{t} (and, moreover, the Riemannian metric ωt(⋅,It⋅)\omega_{t}(\cdot,I_{t}\cdot) is Ricci-flat) for each t∈[0,1]t\in[0,1]. Now Moser’s theorem [Mos] implies that there exists ϕ∈Diff0​(M)\phi\in\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M) such that ϕ∗​ω1=ω\phi^{*}\omega_{1}=\omega.
Consequently, the complex structure ϕ∗​I1\phi^{*}I_{1} is compatible with ω\omega, meaning that [I1]∈𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M,ω)[I_{1}]\in{\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M,\omega).
Hence, W=WI1W=W_{I_{1}} lies in 𝖯𝖾𝗋⁡(𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M,ω))\operatorname{\sf Per}\left({\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M,\omega)\right). This yields 𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M,ω)⊃𝖯𝖾𝗋−1⁡(Dω)∩𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M){\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M,\omega)\supset\operatorname{\sf Per}^{-1}({\it D}_{\omega})\cap{\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M).
We have shown that 𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M,ω)=𝖯𝖾𝗋−1⁡(Dω)∩𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M){\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M,\omega)=\operatorname{\sf Per}^{-1}({\it D}_{\omega})\cap{\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M). Since, by part A of 10.2, 𝖯𝖾𝗋:𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M)→G​r+⁣+​(H2​(M,ℝ))\operatorname{\sf Per}:{\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M)\to Gr_{++}(H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{R}}))
is a surjective local biholomorphism which is injective on a dense connected subset of 𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M){\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M) and since, by 10.2, Dω{\it D}_{\omega} is a dense connected manifold of a closed submanifold of G​r+⁣+​(H2​(M,ℝ))=𝖯𝖾𝗋⁡(𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M))Gr_{++}\left(H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{R}})\right)=\operatorname{\sf Per}\left({\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M)\right),
we get that 𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M,ω){\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M,\omega) is a dense connected subset of the closed submanifold 𝖯𝖾𝗋−1⁡(G​r+⁣+​([ω]⊥))∩𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M)\operatorname{\sf Per}^{-1}\left(Gr_{++}\left([\omega]^{\bot}\right)\right)\cap{\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M) of 𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M){\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M).
Proposition 10.19
The group SympH​(M,ω)\mathrm{Symp}_{H}(M,\omega) acts transitively on the set of connected components of 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M) compatible with ω\omega.
Proof of 10.2:
Assume 𝒞0{\mathcal{C}}_{0} and 𝒞1{\mathcal{C}}_{1} are connected components of 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M) compatible with ω\omega. We need to show that there exists a symplectomorphism
in SympH​(M,ω)\mathrm{Symp}_{H}(M,\omega) mapping 𝒞1{\mathcal{C}}_{1} into 𝒞0{\mathcal{C}}_{0}.
By 10.2,
𝖯𝖾𝗋⁡(𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M,ω))=𝖯𝖾𝗋⁡(𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞1​(M,ω))=Dω.\operatorname{\sf Per}\left({\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M,\omega)\right)=\operatorname{\sf Per}\left({\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{1}}(M,\omega)\right)={\it D}_{\omega}.
Pick I0∈𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M,ω)∩𝒮I_{0}\in{\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M,\omega)\cap{\mathcal{S}}, I1∈𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞1​(M,ω)∩𝒮I_{1}\in{\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{1}}(M,\omega)\cap{\mathcal{S}},
so that 𝖯𝖾𝗋⁡([I0])=𝖯𝖾𝗋⁡([I1])\operatorname{\sf Per}([I_{0}])=\operatorname{\sf Per}([I_{1}]). By part B of 10.2, there exists ϕ∈DiffH​(M)\phi\in\mathrm{Diff}_{H}(M) such that ϕ∗​I1=I0\phi^{*}I_{1}=I_{0}.
Then ϕ∗​ω\phi^{*}\omega and ω\omega are cohomologous and compatible with the same complex structure I0I_{0}. Therefore the straight path in the space of 2-forms connecting these forms is formed by cohomologous symplectic forms. By Moser’s theorem [Mos], this means that there exists ψ∈Diff0​(M)\psi\in\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M) such that
ψ∗​ϕ∗​ω=ω\psi^{*}\phi^{*}\omega=\omega. Thus, ϕ​ψ∈SympH​(𝕋2​n,ω)\phi\psi\in\mathrm{Symp}_{H}({\mathbb{T}}^{2n},\omega),
and moreover (ϕ​ψ)∗​I1=ψ∗​ϕ∗​I1(\phi\psi)^{*}I_{1}=\psi^{*}\phi^{*}I_{1} lies in the same connected component of 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M) as I0=ϕ∗​I1I_{0}=\phi^{*}I_{1} – i.e., in 𝒞0{\mathcal{C}}_{0}.
Thus, the symplectomorphism ϕ​ψ∈SympH​(M,ω)\phi\psi\in\mathrm{Symp}_{H}(M,\omega) maps 𝒞1{\mathcal{C}}_{1} into 𝒞0{\mathcal{C}}_{0}.
Proposition 10.20
The Kähler-type symplectic form ω\omega is compatible with a Campana-simple complex structure on MM if and only if
ω\omega is irrational.
Moreover, in this case for any connected component 𝒞0{\mathcal{C}}_{0} of
𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M) compatible with ω\omega the set
𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M,ω)∩𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑s​(M){\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M,\omega)\cap{\tt Teich}^{s}(M)
is a dense connected subset of 𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M,ω){\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M,\omega).
Proof of 10.2:
Assume ω\omega is rational.
Then, by part 1 of
9, there are no Campana-simple complex structures compatible with ω\omega.
Now assume ω\omega is irrational. Let us show that
the set
of Campana-simple structures lying in 𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M,ω){\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M,\omega)
is a dense connected subset of 𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M,ω){\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M,\omega).
Let b∈H2​(M,ℚ)b\in H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{Q}}), b≠0b\neq 0. Since [ω]∈H2​(M,ℝ)[\omega]\in H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{R}}) is not a real multiple of a rational cohomology class (and, in particular, not a real multiple of bb), dimℝb⊥∩[ω]⊥=20\dim_{\mathbb{R}}b^{\bot}\cap[\omega]^{\bot}=20. Consequently, the space G​r+⁣+​(b⊥∩[ω]⊥)Gr_{++}\left(b^{\bot}\cap[\omega]^{\bot}\right) of positive oriented 22-dimensional planes in
b⊥∩[ω]⊥b^{\bot}\cap[\omega]^{\bot} has real codimension 22 in G​r+⁣+​([ω]⊥)Gr_{++}\left([\omega]^{\bot}\right) if q⁡(b,b)<0q(b,b)<0 and is empty otherwise.
As we have seen in the proof of 10.2, Dω{\it D}_{\omega} is the complement in G​r+⁣+​([ω]⊥)Gr_{++}\left([\omega]^{\bot}\right) of an (at most) countable union of closed codimension-22 submanifolds G​r+⁣+​(a⊥∩[ω]⊥)Gr_{++}\left(a^{\bot}\cap[\omega]^{\bot}\right) for all (−2)(-2)-classes a∈H2​(M,ℤ)a\in H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{Z}}) such that q⁡(a,[ω])=0q(a,[\omega])=0. Therefore the complement in Dω{\it D}_{\omega} to the countable union of codimension-2 submanifolds G​r+⁣+​(b⊥∩[ω]⊥)Gr_{++}\left(b^{\bot}\cap[\omega]^{\bot}\right), b∈H2​(M,ℚ)b\in H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{Q}}), b≠0b\neq 0, is a dense connected subset of Dω{\it D}_{\omega}. Denote the latter subset by AA.
By part A of 10.2 and 10.2, 𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M,ω)∩𝖯𝖾𝗋−1⁡(A){\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M,\omega)\cap\operatorname{\sf Per}^{-1}(A) is a dense connected subset of 𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M,ω){\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M,\omega).
We claim that each II such that [I]∈𝖯𝖾𝗋−1⁡(A)∩𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M,ω)[I]\in\operatorname{\sf Per}^{-1}(A)\cap{\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M,\omega) is Campana-simple. Indeed, if (M,I)(M,I) admits a proper positive-dimensional analytic subvariety – i.e., a (possibly singular) complex curve – then the cohomology class b∈H2​(M,ℚ)b\in H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{Q}}), b≠0b\neq 0, Poincaré-dual to the fundamental class of the subvariety is of type (1,1)(1,1) with respect to II (see e.g. [Voi, Vol. 1, Sec. 11]).
Therefore the 2-dimensional plane 𝖯𝖾𝗋⁡([I])∈A\operatorname{\sf Per}([I])\in A is orthogonal to bb in H2​(M,ℝ)H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{R}}) which contradicts the definition of AA.
Thus, 𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M,ω)∩𝖯𝖾𝗋−1⁡(A){\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M,\omega)\cap\operatorname{\sf Per}^{-1}(A) is a dense connected subset of the set
𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M,ω)∩𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑s​(M){\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M,\omega)\cap{\tt Teich}^{s}(M).
Hence, 𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M,ω)∩𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑s​(M){\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M,\omega)\cap{\tt Teich}^{s}(M)
is a dense connected subset of 𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑𝒞0​(M,ω){\tt Teich}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{0}}(M,\omega).
10.3 Kähler-type embeddings of balls into tori and K3 surfaces – the proofs
Now we are ready to prove 3.4. For convenience
we restate it here.
Theorem 10.21 (3.4)
Let MM, dimℝM=2​n\dim_{\mathbb{R}}M=2n, be either 𝕋2​n{\mathbb{T}}^{2n} or a smooth manifold (of real dimension 44) underlying a complex K3 surface
and let ω\omega be a Kähler-type symplectic form on MM.
Assume that ω\omega is irrational.
Let k∈ℤ>0k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}, r1,…,rk>0r_{1},\ldots,r_{k}>0.
Then the following claims hold:
A. Kähler-type embeddings of ⨆i=1kB2​n​(ri)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{2n}(r_{i}) into (M,ω)(M,\omega) are unobstructed.
More precisely, assume that Vol⁡(⨆i=1kB2​n​(ri),ω0)<Vol⁡(M,ω){\rm{Vol}\,}\left(\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{2n}(r_{i}),\omega_{0}\right)<{\rm{Vol}\,}(M,\omega) and ω\omega is
compatible with a Campana-simple complex structure II.
Then
there exists an [I][I]-Kähler-type embedding ⨆i=1kB2​n​(ri)→(M,ω)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{2n}(r_{i})\to(M,\omega).
B.
The group SympH​(M,ω)\mathrm{Symp}_{H}(M,\omega) acts transitively on the set of connected components of
𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M) compatible with ω\omega.
C.
Any two Kähler-type embeddings ⨆i=1kB2​n​(ri)→(M,ω)\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{2n}(r_{i})\to(M,\omega) (if they exist!) lie in the same orbit of the
SympH​(M,ω)\mathrm{Symp}_{H}(M,\omega)-action. They lie in the same orbit of the Symp⁡(M,ω)∩Diff0​(M)\mathrm{Symp}(M,\omega)\cap\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M)-action
if and only if they favor a common connected component of 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M). In the latter case there exists [I]∈𝚃𝚎𝚒𝚌𝚑⁡(M,ω)[I]\in{\tt Teich}(M,\omega)
such that both embeddings are of [I][I]-Kähler-type.
Proof of 10.3 (=3.4):
Let us first consider the torus case.
By 10.1, in this case we may assume without loss of generality that 𝒞0=𝒞l{\mathcal{C}}_{0}={\mathcal{C}}_{l}.
Now part A of the theorem follows from part (I) of 9 and 10.1.
Part B of the theorem is exactly 10.1.
Part C of the theorem follows from part B and from part (II) of 9 and 10.1.
This finishes the proof in the torus case.
Let us now prove the result in the K3 case.
Here part A of the theorem follows from part (I) of 9 and
10.2.
Part B is exactly 10.2.
Part C of the theorem follows from part B and from part (II) of 9.
This finishes the proof in the K3 case.
11 Tame
embeddings of arbitrary domains
In this section we will prove the results on
tame
embeddings into tori and K3 surfaces stated in Section 3.4.
Let MM, dimℝM=2​n\dim_{\mathbb{R}}M=2n, be a closed connected oriented manifold admitting Kähler structures (compatible with the orientation).
Denote by Ωm​(M)\Omega^{m}(M) the space of smooth differential mm-forms on MM, m=0,1,…,2​nm=0,1,\ldots,2n.
Let S⁡(M)\mathit{S}(M) denote the space of symplectic structures on MM
and C⁡(M)\mathit{C}(M) the space of complex structures on MM.
We equip these spaces with the C1C^{1}-topologies.
Let Wi⊂ℝ2​nW_{i}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}, i=1,…,ki=1,\ldots,k, be compact domains with piecewise-smooth boundary, so that their interiors contain the origin and H2​(Wi,ℝ)=0H^{2}(W_{i};{\mathbb{R}})=0.
Denote
𝕎:=⨆i=1kWi.{\mathbb{W}}:=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}W_{i}.
Proposition 11.1
Assume ω\omega is a symplectic form on MM
and
f:𝕎→(M,ω)f:{\mathbb{W}}\to(M,\omega) is a
tame
embedding holomorphic with respect to a complex structure II on MM tamed by ω\omega.
Let 𝒱{\mathcal{V}} be a sufficiently small neighborhood of II in C⁡(M)\mathit{C}(M) so that each I′∈𝒱I^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{V}} is tamed by ω\omega.
Then there exists a neighborhood 𝒰=𝒰⁡(𝒱){\mathcal{U}}={\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{V}}) of ω\omega in S⁡(M)\mathit{S}(M) such that for each ω′∈𝒰\omega^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{U}}
there exists a tame
embedding
f′:𝕎→(M,ω′)f^{\prime}:{\mathbb{W}}\to(M,\omega^{\prime})
holomorphic with respect to a complex structure I′∈𝒱I^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{V}} which is tamed by ω′\omega^{\prime} and isotopic to II.
Proof of 11:
The following lemma is standard – it is proved by an easy modification of the proof of a similar result in [EV1, Lem. 9.3].
Lemma 11.2
Let V⊂MV\subset M, dimℝV=2​n\dim_{\mathbb{R}}V=2n, be a compact
submanifold with a
piecewise-smooth boundary.
Let 𝒳{\mathcal{X}} be a neighborhood of zero in the space Ω1​(M)\Omega^{1}(M).
Then there exists a neighborhood 𝒳′{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime} of zero in the space Ω2​(M)\Omega^{2}(M),
so that for each η∈𝒳′\eta\in{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}, which is exact on VV, one can
choose σ∈𝒳\sigma\in{\mathcal{X}} such that d​σ=ηd\sigma=\eta on VV.
Assume that the tame embedding f:=⨆i=1kfi:𝕎→(M,ω)f:=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}f_{i}:{\mathbb{W}}\to(M,\omega) extends (as a
tame embedding) to an embedding of an open neighborhood of
𝕌=⨆i=1kUi{\mathbb{U}}=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}U_{i}, where UiU_{i}, i=1,…,ki=1,\ldots,k, is a compact domain with a piecewise-smooth boundary containing WiW_{i} in its interior and homotopy equivalent to WiW_{i}, so that
ff is holomorphic (on a neighborhood of 𝕌{\mathbb{U}}) with respect to II.
Choose a neighborhood 𝒰1{\mathcal{U}}_{1} of ω\omega in S⁡(M)\mathit{S}(M) so that for each ω′∈𝒰1\omega^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{U}}_{1} the following conditions are satisfied:
•
For each t∈[0,1]t\in[0,1] the form ω+t⁡(ω−ω′)\omega+t(\omega-\omega^{\prime}) is symplectic.
•
ω′\omega^{\prime} is tamed by any I′∈𝒱I^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{V}}.
Choose a neighborhood 𝒲{\mathcal{W}} of I​dId in the group Diff0​(M)\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M) so that ϕ∗​I∈𝒱\phi_{*}I\in{\mathcal{V}}
for each ϕ∈𝒲\phi\in{\mathcal{W}}.
Choose a neighborhood 𝒴{\mathcal{Y}} of 00 in the space of time-dependent smooth vector fields on MM, equipped with the C1C^{1}-topology, so that for each vector field v∈𝒴v\in{\mathcal{Y}} the time [0,1][0,1]-flow of vv
generates a diffeomorphism lying in 𝒲{\mathcal{W}} and such that
for each t∈[0,1]t\in[0,1] the time-[0,t][0,t] flow of vv maps each f⁡(Wi)f(W_{i}) into f⁡(I​n​t​Ui)f(Int\,U_{i}), i=1,…,ki=1,\ldots,k.
Since for all i=1,…,ki=1,\ldots,k we have H2​(Wi,ℝ)=H2​(Ui,ℝ)=0H^{2}(W_{i};{\mathbb{R}})=H^{2}(U_{i};{\mathbb{R}})=0, for each ω′∈𝒰1\omega^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{U}}_{1} the restriction of the form ω−ω′\omega-\omega^{\prime} to f⁡(𝕌)f({\mathbb{U}}) is exact. Each σ∈Ω1​(M)\sigma\in\Omega^{1}(M)
satisfying d​σ=ω−ω′d\sigma=\omega-\omega^{\prime} defines a smooth time-dependent vector field vv on MM by the formula
(ω+t⁡(ω−ω′))​(vt,⋅)=σ⁡(⋅),t∈[0,1].\left(\omega+t(\omega-\omega^{\prime})\right)(v_{t},\cdot)=\sigma(\cdot),\ t\in[0,1].
In view of 11, applied to f⁡(𝕌)f({\mathbb{U}}), there exists a neighborhood 𝒰2⊂𝒰1{\mathcal{U}}_{2}\subset{\mathcal{U}}_{1} of ω\omega in 𝑆𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{SK}(M) so that for each ω′∈𝒰3\omega^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{U}}_{3} one can choose σ\sigma as above so that the corresponding vv lies in 𝒴{\mathcal{Y}}.
Let ω′∈𝒰2\omega^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{U}}_{2} and let {ϕt}⊂Diff0​(M)\{\phi_{t}\}\subset\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M), t∈[0,1]t\in[0,1], be the time-[0,t][0,t] flow of v∈𝒴v\in{\mathcal{Y}}. Then there exists an open neighborhood Zi⊂I​n​t​UiZ_{i}\subset Int\,\,U_{i} of each WiW_{i}, i=1,…,ki=1,\ldots,k, so that ϕt​(Zi)⊂f⁡(I​n​t​Ui)\phi_{t}(Z_{i})\subset f(Int\,\,U_{i}) for all t∈[0,1]t\in[0,1], i=1,…,ki=1,\ldots,k.
Moser’s argument11
1
Let us note that in the proof of [EV1, Prop. 9.1] we applied Moser’s argument to symplectic forms on the domain of ff, while here we will apply it to symplectic forms on the target of ff – i.e., on MM. [Mos] shows that
ϕ1∗ω′|f⁡(Zi)=ω|f⁡(Zi),i=1,…,k.\phi_{1}^{*}\omega^{\prime}|_{f(Z_{i})}=\omega|_{f(Z_{i})},\ i=1,\ldots,k.
We claim that
g:=ϕ1∘f=⨆i=1kϕ1∘fi:𝕎→(M,ω′)g:=\phi_{1}\circ f=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}\phi_{1}\circ f_{i}:{\mathbb{W}}\to(M,\omega^{\prime})
is a tame embedding.
Indeed, it is clearly a smooth embedding extending to a neighborhood Z:=⨆i=1kZiZ:=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}Z_{i} of 𝕎{\mathbb{W}} so that g⁡(Z)⊂I​n​t​f​(𝕌)g(Z)\subset Int\,f({\mathbb{U}}). It is also symplectic on ℤ{\mathbb{Z}}:
g∗​ω′=(ϕ1∘f)∗​ω′=f∗​ϕ1∗​ω′=f∗​ω=ω0,g^{*}\omega^{\prime}=(\phi_{1}\circ f)^{*}\omega^{\prime}=f^{*}\phi_{1}^{*}\omega^{\prime}=f^{*}\omega=\omega_{0},
where ω0\omega_{0} is the standard symplectic form on ℝ2​n{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}.
Note that since the embedding ff is holomorphic with respect to the complex structure II, the embedding gg is holomorphic with respect to the complex structure J:=(ϕ1)∗​IJ:=(\phi_{1})_{*}I.
By our construction, ϕ1∈𝒲\phi_{1}\in{\mathcal{W}}, hence J∈𝒱J\in{\mathcal{V}}. Since ω′∈𝒰2⊂𝒰1\omega^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{U}}_{2}\subset{\mathcal{U}}_{1}, the complex structure
JJ is tamed by ω′\omega^{\prime}.
This shows that for 𝒰:=𝒰2{\mathcal{U}}:={\mathcal{U}}_{2} and any ω′∈𝒰\omega^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{U}} there exists a tame embedding
g:𝕎→(M,ω′)g:{\mathbb{W}}\to(M,\omega^{\prime})
holomorphic with respect to the complex structure
JJ tamed by ω′\omega^{\prime} and isotopic to II.
Proposition 11.3
Let
ε>0\varepsilon>0.
Assume ω\omega is a Kähler-type symplectic form on MM
and
f:𝕎→(M,ω)f:{\mathbb{W}}\to(M,\omega) is an
ε\varepsilon-tame
embedding holomorphic with respect to a Kähler-type complex structure II on MM tamed by ω\omega.
Then there exists a neighborhood 𝒰{\mathcal{U}} of ω\omega in 𝑆𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{SK}(M) such that for each ω′∈𝒰\omega^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{U}}
there exists an ε\varepsilon-tame
embedding
f′:𝕎→(M,ω′)f^{\prime}:{\mathbb{W}}\to(M,\omega^{\prime})
holomorphic with respect to a complex structure which is tamed by ω′\omega^{\prime} and isotopic to II.
Proof of 11:
Choose a neighborhood 𝒱{\mathcal{V}} of II in 𝐶𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{CK}(M).
Applying 11 to 𝕎{\mathbb{W}}, we get a neighborhood 𝒰{\mathcal{U}} of ω\omega in S⁡(M)\mathit{S}(M)
such that for each ω′∈𝒰\omega^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{U}} there exists a tame
embedding f′:𝕎→(M,ω′)f^{\prime}:{\mathbb{W}}\to(M,\omega^{\prime}) holomorphic with respect to a complex structure I′∈𝒱I^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{V}}
tamed by ω′\omega^{\prime} so that I′=ϕ∗​I∈𝒱I^{\prime}=\phi_{*}I\in{\mathcal{V}} for some ϕ∈Diff0​(M)\phi\in\mathrm{Diff}_{0}(M). In particular, for all p,q∈ℤ≥0p,q\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0},
[ω′]I′p,q=[ω′]ϕ∗​Ip,q=[ω′]Ip,q.[\omega^{\prime}]_{I^{\prime}}^{p,q}=[\omega^{\prime}]_{\phi_{*}I}^{p,q}=[\omega^{\prime}]_{I}^{p,q}.
The cohomology class [ω′][\omega^{\prime}] depends continuously on ω′\omega^{\prime} and the Hodge decomposition (with respect to II) of a cohomology class
depends continuously on that class. Also, the Kähler cone of (M,I)(M,I) is an open subset of H1,1​(M,ℝ)H^{1,1}(M;{\mathbb{R}}).
In view of these facts, we can choose, if necessary, a smaller neighborhood 𝒰{\mathcal{U}} and assume without loss of generality that for any ω′∈𝒰\omega^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{U}}:
•
The class
[ω′]I′1,1=[ω′]I1,1[\omega^{\prime}]_{I^{\prime}}^{1,1}=[\omega^{\prime}]_{I}^{1,1} is Kähler,
•
For a:=[ω]I′2,0+[ω]I′0,2=[ω′]I2,0+[ω′]I0,2a:=[\omega]_{I^{\prime}}^{2,0}+[\omega]_{I^{\prime}}^{0,2}=[\omega^{\prime}]_{I}^{2,0}+[\omega^{\prime}]_{I}^{0,2}, we have |⟨an,[M]⟩|<ε\left|\langle a^{n},[M]\rangle\right|<\varepsilon.
This yields that f′:𝕎→(M,ω′)f^{\prime}:{\mathbb{W}}\to(M,\omega^{\prime}) is an ε\varepsilon-tame symplectic embedding holomorphic with respect to the complex structure I′I^{\prime} which is tamed by ω′\omega^{\prime} and isotopic to II.
Corollary 11.4
Assume ω\omega is a Kähler-type symplectic form on MM
and
f:𝕎→(M,ω)f:{\mathbb{W}}\to(M,\omega) is a Kähler-type embedding
holomorphic with respect to a Kähler-type complex structure II on MM compatible ω\omega.
Then, for any ε>0\varepsilon>0,
there exists a neighborhood 𝒰{\mathcal{U}} of ω\omega in 𝑆𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{SK}(M) such that for each ω′∈𝒰\omega^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{U}}
there exists an ε\varepsilon-tame
embedding
f′:𝕎→(M,ω′)f^{\prime}:{\mathbb{W}}\to(M,\omega^{\prime})
holomorphic with respect to a complex structure which is tamed by ω′\omega^{\prime} and isotopic to II.
Proposition 11.5
Let ε>0\varepsilon>0.
Then the function ω↦νT,ε​(M,ω,𝕎)\omega\mapsto\nu_{T,\varepsilon}(M,\omega,{\mathbb{W}}) on S⁡(M)\mathit{S}(M) is lower
semicontinuous with respect to the C1C^{1}-topology on S⁡(M)\mathit{S}(M).
Proof of 11:
Consider an arbitrary
ω∈S⁡(M)\omega\in\mathit{S}(M).
Let us prove that νT,ε\nu_{T,\varepsilon} is lower semicontinuous
at ω\omega.
Let δ,λ>0\delta,\lambda>0.
Assume f:λ​𝕎→(M,ω)f:\lambda{\mathbb{W}}\to(M,\omega) is an
ε\varepsilon-tame
embedding holomorphic with respect to a Kähler-type complex structure II on MM tamed by ω\omega.
By 11, there exists a neighborhood 𝒰{\mathcal{U}} of ω\omega in 𝑆𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{SK}(M)
such that for each ω′∈𝒰\omega^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{U}}
there exists an ε\varepsilon-tame
embedding
f′:𝕎→(M,ω′)f^{\prime}:{\mathbb{W}}\to(M,\omega^{\prime}).
Choosing, if necessary, a smaller neighborhood 𝒰{\mathcal{U}}, we may assume without loss of generality that
Vol⁡(λ​𝕎,ω0)Vol⁡(M,ω′)>Vol⁡(λ​𝕎,ω0)Vol⁡(M,ω)−δ,∀ω′∈𝒰.\frac{{\rm{Vol}\,}(\lambda{\mathbb{W}},\omega_{0})}{{\rm{Vol}\,}(M,\omega^{\prime})}>\frac{{\rm{Vol}\,}(\lambda{\mathbb{W}},\omega_{0})}{{\rm{Vol}\,}(M,\omega)}-\delta,\ \forall\omega^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{U}}.
This shows that νT,ε\nu_{T,\varepsilon} is lower semicontinuous
at ω\omega.
Thus, νT,ε\nu_{T,\varepsilon} is lower semicontinuous
at all ω∈S⁡(M)\omega\in\mathit{S}(M), meaning that it is a lower semicontinuous function.
Let us now prove 3.4. For convenience, we restate it here.
Theorem 11.6 (= 3.4)
Let MM be either a torus 𝕋2​n{\mathbb{T}}^{2n} or a smooth manifold underlying a K3 surface.
Let ω1\omega_{1}, ω2\omega_{2} be
Kähler-type forms on MM so that
∫Mω1n=∫Mω2n>0\int_{M}\omega_{1}^{n}=\int_{M}\omega_{2}^{n}>0 and the
forms ω1\omega_{1}, ω2\omega_{2} are irrational.
Let Wi⊂ℝ2​nW_{i}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}, i=1,…,ki=1,\ldots,k, be compact domains with piecewise-smooth boundary whose interiors contain the origin.
Assume that H2​(Wi,ℝ)=0H^{2}(W_{i};{\mathbb{R}})=0 for all ii. Set 𝕎:=⨆i=1kWi{\mathbb{W}}:=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}W_{i}.
Then, for
any ε>0\varepsilon>0,
νT,ε​(M,ω1,𝕎)=νT,ε​(M,ω2,𝕎).\nu_{T,\varepsilon}(M,\omega_{1},{\mathbb{W}})=\nu_{T,\varepsilon}(M,\omega_{2},{\mathbb{W}}).
Proof of 11 (= 3.4):
Without loss of generality, it is enough to proof the theorem in case Vol⁡(M,ω1)=Vol⁡(M,ω2)=1{\rm{Vol}\,}(M,\omega_{1})={\rm{Vol}\,}(M,\omega_{2})=1.
Denote by 𝑆𝐾1​(M)\mathit{SK}_{1}(M) the space of Kähler forms on MM of total volume 11. Equip
𝑆𝐾1​(M)\mathit{SK}_{1}(M) with the C∞C^{\infty}-topology. The group Diff+​(M)\mathrm{Diff}^{+}(M) of
orientation-preserving diffeomorphisms of MM acts on 𝑆𝐾1​(M)\mathit{SK}_{1}(M) and the
function ω↦νT,ε​(M,ω,𝕎)\omega\mapsto\nu_{T,\varepsilon}(M,\omega,{\mathbb{W}}) is clearly invariant under the action.
By [EV1, Thm. 9.2] in the torus case and [EV3, Thm. 1.7] in the K3 case, the Diff+​(M)\mathrm{Diff}^{+}(M)-orbits of ω1\omega_{1} and ω2\omega_{2} are dense in 𝑆𝐾1​(M)\mathit{SK}_{1}(M). The function νT,ε​(M,⋅,𝕎)\nu_{T,\varepsilon}(M,\cdot,{\mathbb{W}}) is constant
on both orbits. Together with the lower semicontinuity of νT,ε​(M,⋅,𝕎)\nu_{T,\varepsilon}(M,\cdot,{\mathbb{W}}) proved in 11,
this implies that
νT,ε​(M,ω1,𝕎)≤νT,ε​(M,ω2,𝕎),\nu_{T,\varepsilon}(M,\omega_{1},{\mathbb{W}})\leq\nu_{T,\varepsilon}(M,\omega_{2},{\mathbb{W}}),
and, by the same token,
νT,ε​(M,ω1,𝕎)≥νT,ε​(M,ω2,𝕎).\nu_{T,\varepsilon}(M,\omega_{1},{\mathbb{W}})\geq\nu_{T,\varepsilon}(M,\omega_{2},{\mathbb{W}}).
Thus,
νT,ε​(M,ω1,𝕎)=νT,ε​(M,ω2,𝕎),\nu_{T,\varepsilon}(M,\omega_{1},{\mathbb{W}})=\nu_{T,\varepsilon}(M,\omega_{2},{\mathbb{W}}),
as required. This proves the theorem.
Corollary 11.7
Let MM be either a torus 𝕋2​n{\mathbb{T}}^{2n} or a smooth manifold underlying a K3 surface.
Let Wi⊂ℝ2​nW_{i}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}, i=1,…,ki=1,\ldots,k, be compact domains with piecewise-smooth boundary whose interiors contain the origin.
Assume that H2​(Wi,ℝ)=0H^{2}(W_{i};{\mathbb{R}})=0 for all ii. Set 𝕎:=⨆i=1kWi{\mathbb{W}}:=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}W_{i}.
Let ε>0\varepsilon>0.
Finally, assume that for any δ>0\delta>0 there exists an irrational Kähler-type form
ωδ\omega_{\delta} on MM and λ>0\lambda>0
so that λ​𝕎\lambda{\mathbb{W}} admits a Kähler-type embedding into (M,ωδ)(M,\omega_{\delta})
and Vol⁡(λ​𝕎,ω0)/Vol⁡(M,ωδ)≥1−δ{\rm{Vol}\,}(\lambda{\mathbb{W}},\omega_{0})/{\rm{Vol}\,}(M,\omega_{\delta})\geq 1-\delta.
Then the following claims hold:
A. If ωδ=ω\omega_{\delta}=\omega for all δ>0\delta>0 and ω\omega is irrational, then there exists a dense Diff+​(M)\mathrm{Diff}^{+}(M)-orbit – the Diff+​(M)\mathrm{Diff}^{+}(M)-orbit of ω\omega – in the space of Kähler-type symplectic forms of volume Vol⁡(M,ω){\rm{Vol}\,}(M,\omega) on MM
such that for any ω′\omega^{\prime} in this orbit we have
νK​(M,ω′,𝕎)=1\nu_{K}(M,\omega^{\prime},{\mathbb{W}})=1.
B. There exists a Diff+​(M)\mathrm{Diff}^{+}(M)-invariant open dense set of Kähler-type symplectic forms on MM, depending on 𝕎{\mathbb{W}} and ε\varepsilon and containing, in particular, all irrational Kähler-type symplectic forms on MM,
so that for each ω′\omega^{\prime} in this set
νT,ε​(M,ω′,𝕎)=1\nu_{T,\varepsilon}(M,\omega^{\prime},{\mathbb{W}})=1.
Proof of 11:
Let us prove part A.
The hypothesis of part A implies that νK​(M,ω,𝕎)=1\nu_{K}(M,\omega,{\mathbb{W}})=1.
Since ω\omega is irrational, [EV1, Thm. 9.2] in the torus case and [EV3, Thm. 1.7] in the K3 case yield that the Diff+​(M)\mathrm{Diff}^{+}(M)-orbit of ω\omega is dense in the space of Kähler-type symplectic forms of volume Vol⁡(M,ω){\rm{Vol}\,}(M,\omega) on MM.
Since the function ω′↦νK​(M,ω′,𝕎)\omega^{\prime}\mapsto\nu_{K}(M,\omega^{\prime},{\mathbb{W}}) is Diff+​(M)\mathrm{Diff}^{+}(M)-invariant and νK​(M,ω,𝕎)=1\nu_{K}(M,\omega,{\mathbb{W}})=1,
we have
νK​(M,ω′,𝕎)=1\nu_{K}(M,\omega^{\prime},{\mathbb{W}})=1
for any ω′\omega^{\prime} in the Diff+​(M)\mathrm{Diff}^{+}(M)-orbit of ω\omega.
This yields part A of the corollary.
Let us prove part B.
Rescaling, if necessary, the forms ωδ\omega_{\delta}, we may assume without loss of generality that they all have the same volume – say, volume 11. Since the forms ωδ\omega_{\delta} are all irrational, 11 implies then that νT,ε​(M,ωδ,𝕎)\nu_{T,\varepsilon}(M,\omega_{\delta},{\mathbb{W}}) does not depend on δ\delta. On the other hand, the hypothesis of the corollary implies that νT,ε​(M,ωδ,𝕎)≥1−δ\nu_{T,\varepsilon}(M,\omega_{\delta},{\mathbb{W}})\geq 1-\delta for all δ>0\delta>0. Hence, νT,ε​(M,ωδ,𝕎)=1\nu_{T,\varepsilon}(M,\omega_{\delta},{\mathbb{W}})=1 for all δ>0\delta>0. Using again 11 and rescaling the forms, we get that
νT,ε​(M,ω′,𝕎)=1\nu_{T,\varepsilon}(M,\omega^{\prime},{\mathbb{W}})=1 for any irrational Kähler-type form ω′\omega^{\prime} on MM, of any volume.
By 11, we get νT,ε​(M,ω′′,𝕎)=1\nu_{T,\varepsilon}(M,\omega^{\prime\prime},{\mathbb{W}})=1
also for any Kähler-type form ω′′\omega^{\prime\prime} lying in a sufficiently small neighborhood U⁡(ω′)U(\omega^{\prime}) of any irrational form ω′\omega^{\prime} in 𝑆𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{SK}(M). Let UU be the Diff+​(M)\mathrm{Diff}^{+}(M)-orbit of ⋃ω′U⁡(ω′)\bigcup_{\omega^{\prime}}U(\omega^{\prime}), where the union is taken over all irrational ω′∈𝑆𝐾⁡(M)\omega^{\prime}\in\mathit{SK}(M).
Clearly, UU is a Diff+​(M)\mathrm{Diff}^{+}(M)-invariant open dense subset of 𝑆𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{SK}(M), containing all the irrational forms in 𝑆𝐾⁡(M)\mathit{SK}(M),
and νT,ε​(M,ω′,𝕎)=1\nu_{T,\varepsilon}(M,\omega^{\prime},{\mathbb{W}})=1 holds for any ω′∈U\omega^{\prime}\in U.
This finishes the proof of part B and of the corollary.
Corollary 11.8 (= 3.4)
Assume:
•
MM is either 𝕋2​n{\mathbb{T}}^{2n} or a smooth manifold underlying a K3 surface.
•
𝕎:=⨆i=1kB2​n​(ri){\mathbb{W}}:=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{k}B^{2n}(r_{i}) is a disjoint union of kk (possibly different) balls.
•
ε>0\varepsilon>0.
Then the following claims hold:
A.
For any irrational Kähler-type symplectic form ω\omega on MM we have
νK​(M,ω,𝕎)=1,\nu_{K}(M,\omega,{\mathbb{W}})=1,
meaning that Kähler-type embeddings of λ​𝕎\lambda{\mathbb{W}} into (M,ω)(M,\omega) are
unobstructed.
B. There exists a Diff+​(M)\mathrm{Diff}^{+}(M)-invariant open dense set of Kähler-type symplectic forms on MM, depending on 𝕎{\mathbb{W}} and ε\varepsilon and containing, in particular, all irrational Kähler-type symplectic forms on MM,
so that for each ω\omega in this set
νT,ε​(M,ω,𝕎)=1\nu_{T,\varepsilon}(M,\omega,{\mathbb{W}})=1 – meaning that ε\varepsilon-tame embeddings of λ​𝕎\lambda{\mathbb{W}} into (M,ω)(M,\omega) are
unobstructed.
Proof of 11
(=3.4):
Assume without loss of generality that
n>1n>1. (In the case n=1n=1 the result is known – see 3.4).
Part A of the corollary is equivalent to the existence claim proved in 3.4.
Part B of the corollary follows then from part B of 11.
Now let us prove 3.4. We recall it here.

ε>0\varepsilon>0.

Proof of 11 (=3.4):

n>1n>1. (In the case n=1n=1 the result is known – see 3.4).
Let us consider the case where 𝕎{\mathbb{W}} is the disjoint union of kk identical copies of the polydisk

Note that

k1:=k,k2=…=kl:=1,k_{1}:=k,k_{2}=\ldots=k_{l}:=1,
so that
k=k1⋅…⋅kl.k=k_{1}\cdot\ldots\cdot k_{l}.

Now 11 yield the claims of parts A and B
of 11
in the case where 𝕎{\mathbb{W}} is the disjoint union of kk equal polydisks.
Now let us consider the case where 𝕎{\mathbb{W}} is the disjoint union of kk identical copies of
a parallelepiped
P(e1,…,e2​n):={∑j=12​nsjej,0≤sj≤1,j=1,…,2n},P(e_{1},\ldots,e_{2n}):=\left\{\sum_{j=1}^{2n}s_{j}e_{j},0\leq s_{j}\leq 1,j=1,\ldots,2n\right\},

Note that for any 0<λ<10<\lambda<1, putting kk copies of λ​P​(e1,e2,…,e2​n)\lambda P(e_{1},e_{2},\ldots,e_{2n}), alongside each other in the e1e_{1}-direction we get a Kähler-type embedding of the disjoint union of kk identical copies of λ​P​(e1,e2,…,e2​n)\lambda P(e_{1},e_{2},\ldots,e_{2n}) into P⁡(k​e1,e2,…,e2​n)P(ke_{1},e_{2},\ldots,e_{2n}). (i.e., for the case where k=1k=1).
Therefore let us prove the corollary for one parallelepiped P⁡(e1,…,e2​n)P(e_{1},\ldots,e_{2n}).
Consider the 2​n×2​n2n\times 2n-matrix Z⁡(e1,e2,…,e2​n)Z(e_{1},e_{2},\ldots,e_{2n}) whose columns are

For each m∈ℤ>0m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0} let 𝑀𝑎𝑡m​(ℝ){\it Mat}_{m}({\mathbb{R}}), respectively 𝑀𝑎𝑡m​(ℚ){\it Mat}_{m}({\mathbb{Q}}), be the space of real, respectively rational, m×mm\times m-matrices.

λ​Zt​(0In−In0)​Z∉𝑀𝑎𝑡2​n​(ℚ).\lambda Z^{t}\begin{pmatrix}0&I_{n}\\
